## Document 028: 20261013_投資週報_標普指數獲利預期上修，科技股仍為主要動能

金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 10 月 13 日
上週重點訊息
◼ TOPIX 啟動大規模改革：日本交易所集團 10 月 7 日公布 TOPIX 調整結果，符合新制納入標
準的成分股將由 9 月底的 1,636 檔降至 986 檔，另有 35 檔股票新納入。現有成分股中，共
有 683 檔未符合流動性及浮動市值等標準，這些股票將自 2026 年 10 月底起逐步降低權
重，並於 2027 年 10 月重新評估，屆時仍未達標者將於 2028 年 7 月底降至零。此次改革有
助於改善 TOPIX 成分股的流動性及投資價值，並促使企業更加重視市場評價。

◼ 巴西總統選舉波索納洛首輪領先魯拉：巴西最高選舉法院 10 月 5 日公布首輪投票結果，參
議員弗拉維奧・波索納洛取得 47.03%的有效票，現任總統魯拉獲得 45.16%，兩人均未取得
過半票數，將於 10 月 25 日進行第二輪決選。若最終由波索納洛當選將相較有利於巴西股市
表現。
◼ IEA 支持加速釋出既有儲油：國際能源署（IEA）10 月 7 日表示，會員國支持加快釋出 3 月
已承諾提供的儲油，並將優先增加柴油供應。截至目前，會員國已釋出約 3.25 億桶儲油；若
剩餘承諾全數執行，可再向市場提供約 1 億桶供應，有助緩解運輸業與農業面臨的燃料成本
壓力。隨各國持續釋出戰略儲備石油，將有利於在短期內壓抑全球油價進一步上行。
市場展望
◼ 標普 500 獲利預估連續上修，資訊科技為主要推升來源：標普 500 企業獲利預估連續兩季上
修，其中資訊科技產業為主要改善來源，反映 AI、雲端運算及資料中心需求仍然強勁。雖然
美國資料中心仍面臨電力與基礎設施瓶頸，但建置規模持續擴大，AI 投資也逐步延伸至記憶

體、網路設備、資安及企業應用等領域。因此，預期科技類股的獲利動能仍具支撐，具 AI 供
應鏈優勢的台股與日股亦可望延續相關成長動能。
◼ 中天期債券可望兼顧息收與利率風險：美國經濟仍具韌性，加上政府與企業發債供給維持高
檔，可能使市場利率維持在較高水準。雖然投資級債信用利差位於相對低檔，但企業獲利、
現金流及償債能力仍具支撐，較高的美國公債基準利率也使其利息收益維持一定水準。短天
期債券面臨利率下降後的再投資風險，長天期債券則較易受到公債供給及利率波動影響，因
此中天期美國公債及投資級債可望在息收與利率風險之間維持較為平衡的表現。

國巨* (2327 TT) 
淡季不淡，第四季續強

永豐觀點 
AI 相關占比達 20%，較上季提升 4%。稼動率方面，將較上季 95%持續成長，拋
開過往明顯淡旺季，營運成長可期。 
 投資評價與建議 
維持買進投資建議：主係看好國巨營運將受惠於(1) 日韓廠商將低階 MLCC 產能
移轉至高階產品的訂單外溢效應，(2) 預估 4Q26 稼動率逾 95%。另一方面，漲價
效應浮現將進一步推升毛利率表現， (3) AI 伺服器電源規格提升下，國巨在 AI 
Server 產品線齊全，除 MLCC 外，鉭質電容、電感及晶片電阻的用量增加。綜上
所述，國巨受惠轉單效應、被動元件產業循環向上、仍有持續漲價機會，故維持買
進的投資建議，以 2027-2028 年平均 EPS 預估，給予 27 倍本益比，目標價 940
元。

ESG 評析 
國巨企業永續評鑑整體為 B+等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向平
均得分分別為 B+等與 B+等。 
SinoPac+ ESG 評鑑系統評等

資料來源：SinoPac+ ESG 評等 (依循 2024 SinoPac+企業永續評鑑方法學)

國巨集團為台灣被動元件第一大供應商：3Q26 營收產品別占比 MLCC 21.5%，
Magnetics 22.3%，T antalum(Kemet) 23.7%，Resistor 15.3%，Sensor 10.6%，
Other Components 6.6%，其中 MLCC 與電阻成長主要來自標準品與特殊品需求
強勁 ； 終端應用占比 ：Industrial 28.7%，Computing & Enterprise System 28.3%，
Automotive 14.9%，Consumer 11.7%，T elecom 12.5%，Aerospace/Defense 
/Medical 3.9%，其中 AI 佔比約為 20%，較上季增加 4%。 
價格調漲及稼動率提升，3Q26 獲利優於預期：受惠於 AI 相關及工業 Industrial
需求強勁，帶動各產品線、應用市場營收成長，其中標準品及特殊品皆成長，AI 相
關營收比重達 19.8%。國巨 3Q26 營收 459.32 億元(+11.4% QoQ，+49.7% YoY)，
受惠產品組合及價格調整，毛利率 41%(+2.5pts QoQ，+4.8pts YoY)，優於預期
的 39.5%，營利率 29.5%(+3.1ptsQoQ，+6.6ptsYoY)，稅後淨利 119.8 億元
(+27.2%QoQ，+88.5%YoY)，稅後 EPS 為 5.83 元，優於預期的 5.27 元。 
動能延續，4Q26 規模經濟效應顯現：展望第四季，國巨的 B/B Ratio 約為 2，仍
處於歷史高檔水準。稼動率方面，預將優於上季的 95%。在稼動率持續提升且先
前與客戶的漲價協議陸續發酵下 ， 研究部預估國巨4Q26 營收 532.44 億元(+7.5% 
QoQ，+48% YoY)，受惠產品組合及價格調整，毛利率 41.5%(+0.5pts QoQ ，
+4.2pts YoY)，營利 29.8%(+0.3ptsQoQ，+5.5ptsYoY)，稅後淨利 132.39 億元
(+10.5%QoQ，+96.1%YoY)， 稅後EPS 為 6.45 元 。 預估國巨2026 年營收 1853.98
億（+39.5%YoY） ，毛利率40%(+3.8pts YoY)，營利率28%(+5.6pts YoY)，稅後淨
利 426.4 億元(+80.4%YoY)，稅後 EPS 為 20.76 元。 
產業循環向上空間可期：在 AI 伺服器電源規格不斷提升下，被動元件用量明顯增
加，日韓廠商皆發出漲價通知，國巨受惠於高階 MLCC 產品用量的增加外，鋁質
及鉭質電容等用量增加顯著。此外，預期在轉單效應顯現後，將使國巨在中低階產
品具有市場主導權。若以應用別區分，目前以 AI 相關終端需求最為強勁，國巨產
品亦將受惠鉭質電容 、 電感及晶片電阻的用量增加 ； 車用亦有復甦 。 展望2027 年，
國巨將於 2Q27 陸續開出新產能，預估總產能將年增 10-15%。此外，除 AI 相關
需求持續暢旺外，因競爭對手產能調整，產生的產能缺口難在短期補足，轉單效應
將至少延續至 2Q27，被動元件產業循環仍在持續向上，預期2027 年 MLCC 仍有
30%以上的漲價空間，其餘被動元件也有望因產能排擠使供給出現缺口，帶動下波
漲價 ， 並透過與客戶簽訂長期合約確保營運延續性 ， 故預估國巨2027 年營收 2422.2
億（+30.6%YoY），毛利率43.6%(+3.6pts YoY)，營利率 32%(+4pts YoY)，稅後

### Page 6

Chroma ATE (2360.TW)
30 October 2026 Citi Research
6
Chroma ATE
Company description
Chroma ATE is a leading supplier of testing and instrumentation equipment,
automated testing systems (ATS), and turnkey solutions. Its products are
used for testing power electronics, semiconductor, IC, EV component,
display, and other electronics applications.
Investment strategy
We rate Chroma Buy as we expect structural and robust growth ahead, driven
by: 1) strong power tester demand for AI data center/servers; 2) expanding SLT

(system level testing) demand driven by new generation model launch and
new customers ramp-up; 3) strong metrology growth from increasing foundry
capex investment in advanced packaging; and 4) solid silicon photonics
equipment demand, with CPO a key incremental upside.
Valuation
Our target price of NT$2,900 is based on 40x 2027E P/E, at close to 1x PEG.
Chroma shares have re-rated above its historical range as its growth outlook
is now strongly tied to AI infrastructure build out. We estimate 60%+ of its

revenue are now AI-related, with strong growth across product segments
including power testers, SLT, metrology, and photonics. Chroma has a
commanding market share in most of these segments and well positioned to
capitalized on the global AI expansion.
Risks
Downside risks to achieving our target price include: 1) softer AI/CPO growth
expectation due to changes in capex outlook or production bottleneck; 2)
fewer SLT needed as a result of shortened cycle time as GPU yield rate

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
In this report, we focus on China's manufacturing PMI, which we
see as a potential catalyst for AirTAC's and Hiwin's share prices.
China's manufacturing PMI fell to 49.3 in October from 50.1 in September:

• This came in lower than both our estimate and consensus of 49.9.
• The production index decreased 1.9ppt while the new order index declined
1.1ppt MoM.
Our view:
• We believe the soft manufacturing PMI data could weigh on market
sentiment in the near term. Nonetheless, we prefer supply players with
company-specific drivers such as Airtac and Hiwin:
° Airtac (1590.TW, OW) should continue to benefit from its own share
gains and incremental contribution from linear guides. Valuation appears

undemanding at 16x 2027e P/E, vs. the 25x average since 2020 (see our
30 Oct 2026 report).
° Hiwin (2049.TW, OW) should show more margin expansion in coming
quarters, supported by higher utilization and price hikes. We see
potential share price upside at the current 28x 2027e P/E, vs. peak cycle
valuation of 35-40x.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
AirTAC International (1590.TW)
Base case, 29x 2027e P/E. We believe this methodology better captures earnings growth
momentum for industrial automation component plays (market sentiment moves along
with business cycles). As the industry enters an up-cycle, we think applying a near-peak cycle
valuation of 29x P/E is reasonable given the stronger business outlook.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger macroeconomic climate in China

n Stronger-than-expected demand from industrial automation
Risks to Downside
n Sharper economic downturn in China
n Miniature linear guideway business development taking more time
n Larger-than-expected investment in new products but weaker-than-expected demand
Hiwin Technologies Corp. (2049.TW)
Base case, P/E. We think this valuation methodology better captures Hiwin's earnings growth
momentum, as its valuation tends to move with the business cycle. We apply a target multiple

of 38x to our 2027 EPS estimate. We view this multiple as justified by the 47% operating profit
CAGR we project for 2025-28.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger industrial automation demand pickup.
n Market share gains.
n Earlier-than-expected revenue contribution from humanoids.
Risks to Downside
n Weaker industrial automation demand.
n More pricing pressure.
n Market share loss.
n More broad-based impact on the global economy from geopolitical risks.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

## Document 159: 261104_daiwa_guc

Source PDF: all report pdf/261104_daiwa_guc.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-04
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: Following its results release on 30 October, we raise our 
forecasts and 12M TP for Global Unichip (GUC). The stock more than 
doubled since our last update (Upgrading: unleashed capacity adding to 
growth, 5 August), yet its recent pullback amid increased market volatility 
looks to be unlocking value again for investment, so we reaffirm our Buy (1) 
rating. Fundamentals have unfolded strongly. According to our market 
research, GUC’s key customer in the AI CPU space has pulled forward its 
order trajectory ahead of the previous schedule amid accelerated demand 
for internal and external AI datacentre build-out. This comes on top of its 
initial ramp of another new accelerator project, followed by the next 
generation AI CPU and accelerator ramps for the same customer, with 
further upside from another new AI accelerator customer in the auto space. 
We suggest investors take advantage of the pullback to bargain-hunt.

What’s the impact: Forecast changes. In light of its key AI CPU customer 
accelerating its ordering trajectory, together with multiple AI projects 
ramping in the next 1-2 years to fire on all cylinders for growth, we lift our 
2026-28E EPS by 8-50% for GUC. We forecast triple-digit % YoY EPS 
growth over 2Q26-2Q27, before moderating to 50% or so into 2028. While 
such strong growth should help sustain its hefty valuation, further rerating 
may be unlikely as this would depend on GUC’s ability to win more AI 
projects than we expect, and more importantly, whether it is able to secure 
enough capacity from the supply chain to accommodate any incremental 
business amid the constrained supply environment.

Margin-accretive at operating level. On top of the AI CPU demand uplift, 
we expect GUC to have a total of 6 major AI projects in mass-production for 
2027, including 4 CPU and 2 accelerators, all for US-based hyperscalers or 
system vendors mainly focusing on N3 process nodes, helping broaden 
GUC’s AI project base. Although these projects appear margin-dilutive at 
gross level, they should be accretive at the operating level, thanks to 
GUC’s prudent opex management on enhanced operating leverage.

What we recommend: Given our forecast changes, we lift our 12M TP for 
GUC to TWD7,800 (previous: TWD5,125), based on a higher 4-quarter 
forward PER target of 67x (previous: 63x), justified by its 2026-28E EPS 
CAGR of 66% from a PEG perspective. Although further rerating remains 
challenging, we are comfortable with our Buy (1) call due to the recent 
share price pullback unlocking value for investment again. Key downside 
risks to our call would be supply constraints and margin execution.

How we differ: Our 2027-28E EPS are 23-37% below the consensus 
forecasts as we factor in the supply constraints that will moderate growth.

4 November 2026

Value returns when jitters rise

➢ Lifting forecasts on AI strength and a broadened project base 
➢ Margin dilutive at gross level yet accretive at operating level 
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) with a higher 12M TP of TWD7,800

Company profile 
Founded in 1998, GUC is an IC design service provider focusing on AI/HPC, networking, consumer and 
industrial applications.

### Page 5

5

Global Unichip (3443 TT): 4 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 5 May 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 1
GUC's Board of Directors (BoD) comprises 8 members, including 4 independent directors (50%
of the board), which is significantly higher than the regulatory requirement of at least 20%. The
Board demonstrates diverse expertise across legal, finance, technology, and business
management. The Chairman and the President are different individuals, ensuring separation of
roles and enhancing governance independence.
Capital management 1
Over the past decade, GUC has maintained a consistently high dividend payout ratio, generally
above 60%, and in most years exceeding 80%. In 2025, the company distributed TWD2,144m in
cash dividends, reflecting its policy of sustaining or increasing dividend amounts over time.
Given GUC’s asset-light business model as an IC design service provider, which does not
require significant capital expenditure for manufacturing facilities, this high payout ratio is
appropriate and aligns with its long-term commitment to shareholder returns.
Related party &
transaction 1
GUC's sole foundry supplier is TSMC, who is also GUC's largest shareholder. As an IC design
service company without onsite production, GUC's relationship with the leading foundry of
advanced process nodes provides it a key competitive advantage, in our view.
S Supply chain management 2
GUC is committed to maintaining long-term partnerships with suppliers and establishing a
sustainable supply chain through measures such as promoting its green action policy and
concept to the entire supply chain, where all its suppliers are required to follow its Responsible
Business Alliance (RBA) regulation. It also periodically conducts supplier audits to ensure the
quality of suppliers.
E Water & wastewater management 1
GUC continues its green living project to reduce water consumption. In 2024, the Company
maintained water-saving measures such as rainwater collection systems, automatic sensor
faucets, dual-flush toilets, and regular inspections of water equipment. These efforts aim to
improve water efficiency and promote conservation across operations.
E
Waste & hazardous
materials
management
1
As an IC design service provider, GUC does not have in-house manufacturing facilities. It
requires its suppliers to use recycled materials for on-site operation and transportation, and
reduce the amount of expendable resources being used. In order to promote a paperless office,
GUC has established a cloud operating system to reduce the use of paper-based documents.
E GHG emissions 1
GUC adopts energy-saving measures to reduce greenhouse gas emissions and has set annual
electricity conservation targets. In 2024, the Company achieved a 1% reduction in electricity use
and increased green electricity to 39% of total consumption. It also continues to develop low-
power and high-efficiency IP to help minimise environmental impact.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

3Q26 Review: 악재의 중첩,

최정환 CFA

jeonghwan.choi@daishin.com

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지 
6개월 목표주가 1,640,000 
유지  
현재주가 
(26.10.29) 
739,000 
방위산업

◼ 투자의견 매수, 목표주가 1,640,000원 유지 
◼ 컨센서스 및 추정치 대비 매출, 영업이익 하회 
◼ 4Q26 및 27년 실적 추정치 상향

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 1,640,000원 유지 
4Q26E-2Q27E 지상방산 및 항공우주 EBITDA에 Target 12MF EV/EBITDA 25.0x 
적용, 상장 자회사 가치 SOTP 통해 목표주가 산출. 3Q26 실적 아쉬우나 투자포
인트에는 변함 없어 멀티플은 변경 없이 적용. 27년 실적 추정치 상향에 따라 본
업 가치 상승, 26년 스페인 자주포 양산 및 미국 자주포 시제 사업자로 선정되며 
현지화 전략이 유효했음을 증명. 3Q26 실적에 대한 우려를 소화하며 글로벌 피어
들과의 멀티플 격차 축소를 전망함

컨센서스 및 추정치 대비 매출, 영업이익 하회 
3Q26 연결 기준 매출액 6.1조원(-6.0% YoY), 영업이익 4,520억원(-46.2% YoY, 
OPM 7.4%)으로 컨센서스인 매출액 7.4조원 및 영업이익 1.1조원을 크게 하회
하는 실적 시현. 하회의 주요 요인으로는 3Q26 필리조선소 적자폭 확대, 지상
방산 부문 실적이 부진한 영향. 지상방산 부문의 경우 매출액 1.2조원(-41.0% 
YoY), 영업이익 220억원(-96.2% YoY, OPM 1.7%)을 시현했는데, 폴란드 관련 
매출이 부재했던 1Q26 영업이익률 16.9%보다도 낮은 실적을 보임. 이는 국내
외 사업 모두에서 악재가 겹쳐 발생했던 것으로 보이는데 국내 사업에서는 마
진율이 낮은 사업이 매출로 인식되었고 수출 사업에서는 폴란드 매출 부재 뿐 
아니라 이집트, 호주 K9 자주포 매출 인식 또한 예상 대비 부진해 고정비 레버
리지 효과로 3Q26 영업이익률이 크게 훼손된 것으로 추정. 이외 대전사업장 복
구에 따른 비용, 기타 사업 충당금 등 총 500억원 수준의 일회성 비용이 발생했
으며 영업외손익에서는 원화 강세에 따른 환손실이 주로 반영되었음

4Q26 및 27년 실적 추정치 상향 
1Q26에서 3Q26까지 이뤄지지 않았던 폴란드 EC2 K9 자주포 30문 인도는 
4Q26에 집중될 것으로 보이며 폴란드 EC1 천무 또한 기존 가이던스였던 26년 
천무 40대에서 60대로 인도 대수 상향이 이뤄짐. 이에 따라 4Q26 지상방산 영
업이익률은 22.8%로 회복을 전망. 27년에는 폴란드 K9 자주포 및 천무 인도 대
수 증가로 26년 수출 비중 58.0%에서 77.2%로 증가, 이에 따라 영업이익률 또
한 매출 믹스 개선에 따라 19.8%에서 28.0%로 개선을 전망

## Document 237: [012450] 한화에어로스페이스(012450) 여전히 비중확대 의견, 그리고 Top-Pick한화에어로스페이스(012450) 여전히 비중확대 의견, 그리고 Top-Pick_cd366e43

Source PDF: all report pdf/[012450] 한화에어로스페이스(012450) 여전히 비중확대 의견, 그리고 Top-Pick한화에어로스페이스(012450) 여전히 비중확대 의견, 그리고 Top-Pick_cd366e43.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

(012450) 
한화에어로스페이스 
2026.10.30

AI로보틱스/방산/조선/운송 양승윤 
6139 / syyang0901@eugenefn.com

여전히 비중확대 의견, 그리고 Top-Pick 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 1,660,000원(유지) 
 현재주가 760,000원(10/29)

 3Q26 연결 매출액은 6조 943억원(-6.0%yoy), 영업이익은 4,520억원(-
46.2%yoy, OPM 7.4%)을 기록. 영업이익 컨센 1조 1,614억원을 하회.  
 지상방산의 손익이 크게 악화. 영업이익은 217억원(OPM 1.7%) 기록. 주요 
납품이 4분기에 집중된 가운데 일회성 비용 약 500억원(대전사업장 복구·
정상화 약 300억원, 국내 사업 충당금 약 160억원)과 환율 영향이 겹침.   
 다만 내용을 살펴보면 수출 사업의 수익성 훼손은 크지 않았다는 판단임.  
 일회성 제외 영업이익은 약 717억원(OPM 5.8%)이며, 일회성 비용은 대전
사업장이 국내 물량을 전담한다는 점에서 전액 국내 귀속으로 추정. 내수 
OPM을 일회성 제외 -5% 내외로 가정하면, 내수 영업이익은 약 -826억원
(일회성 포함 OPM -12.7%), 수출은 1,043억원(OPM 17.6%)으로 추정됨. 
 1Q26 대비로는 국내 마진 하락이, 2Q26 대비로는 수출 물량 감소가 가장 
크게 작용(도표 참고). 국내는 저마진 사업 매출이 늘고 고마진 반복 양산 물
량이 줄며 일시적으로 믹스가 악화. 수출은 폴란드 등 주요 인도가 정체되어 
매출이 감소한 가운데 저수익 사업 중심으로 믹스가 악화된 영향. 
 4Q26부터 회복 구간 진입 전망. 폴란드 K9 2차 실행계약 물량의 현지 인
도가 본격화되며, 회사는 2026년 폴란드 K9 30문 이상, 천무 발사대 60대 
이상(*연초 40대에서 상향) 인도를 계획. 수출입 데이터상으로도 폴란드·루
마니아향 출하가 대규모로 진행 중이며 폴란드향 부속류 수출도 정상적으로 
이어지는 것으로 확인. 일회성 소멸과 고마진 수출 집중으로 수출 OPM은 
30% 내외로 회복할 것. 다만 국내는 정상 가동(2027년 상반기 목표) 전까
지 한 자릿수 초반 마진에 머물 것으로 전망. 
 중장기 수익성 우려도 크지 않다는 판단. 2027~2029년 매출 연 15% 성
장 가이던스는 유지됐고, 내년 폴란드 K9·천무 인도 물량은 모두 올해 대비 
증가할 계획. 폴란드 사업 이후 높아진 수출 마진 레벨은 후속 사업에서도 
유지될 전망. 수주는 중동 지정학 불안으로 지연되고 있으나 대공체계 등 문
의가 이어지고 있어, 2027년부터 중동이 수주의 중심이 될 것으로 기대. 
 동사에 대한 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 166만원을 유지함. 3Q26 지상방산 
쇼크는 국내 적자와 수출 물량 공백이 겹친 일시적 결과로, 수출 경쟁력 훼
손으로 보기 어려움. 실적 충격에 따른 주가 조정은 분할 매수 기회로 판단.  
 한화에어로스페이스의 주가는 4월 7일 사상 최고가 153.7만원에서 10월 
29일 76.0만원으로 -51% 조정 받은 상황. 12MF P/E 기준 14.3x, 27E 
P/E는 13.9x로 밸류에이션 관점에서도 매력적인 구간 진입. 그동안 3분기 
실적 우려 등으로 부진했다면, 이제는 실적 바텀아웃과 대형 수주 랠리 속 
다시 한 번 반등을 기록할 차례. 동사를 방산업종 Top-pick으로 유지함.

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

투자기간 및 투자등급/투자의견 비율 
종목추천 및 업종추천 투자기간: 12개월  (추천기준일 종가대비 추천종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함) 당사 투자의견 비율(%)

## Document 257: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 하방보다 상방을 볼 시점HD현대중공업(329180) 하방보다 상방을 볼 시점하방보다 상방을 볼 시점 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 하방보다 상방을 볼 시점HD현대중공업(329180) 하방보다 상방을 볼 시점하방보다 상방을 볼 시점 - 기업.pdf

투자의견 BUY

목표주가 540,000 
하향  
현재주가 
(26.10.28) 
285,500 
조선업종

◼ 글로벌 피어 평균 멀티플 하락을 반영하여 목표주가 하향 
◼ 3Q에 산재한 일회성 요인. 원화강세, 조업일수 감소, 임단협 비용 반영 
◼ 모두의 우려에도 불구하고 본업은 안정적으로 천천히 잘 진행되는 중

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 540,000원으로 -32.5% 하향 
목표주가는 28F EPS 42,343원에 글로벌 조선사 피어 평균 12.8배를 적용해 
산출함. 목표주가 하향은 글로벌 경기 불확실성과 중국 조선소 생산캐파 확장
으로 인한 공급과잉 리스크로 전반적인 산업 멀티플이 하락한 것을 반영함. 
다만 가스선 수주 모멘텀과 발전용 엔진의 수주 및 생산 확대 등에 따른 중장
기 성장 유효

3분기는 원래 비수기입니다 
동사의 2026년 3분기 매출액은 5조 7,536억원(+30.2% YoY, -9.1% QoQ), 영
업이익은 8,992억원(+61.3% YoY, -13.5% QoQ), OPM 15.6%를 기록. 3분기는 
1)원달러 환율 하락(전분기 대비 평균 -79원) 2)연휴로 인한 조업일수 감소(47
일), 3)임단협 관련 추가 발생 가능 비용 75% 반영)의 영향이 가장 컸음

중장기적인 모멘텀은 여전히 살아있다 
[상선] 중국 공급 확대에 따른 선가 및 수주 경쟁 심화는 부담이나 LNG선 발
주 재개 및 노후선 교체 수요는 긍정적. 동사의 고부가가치선 위주의 수주 전
략 방향은 유지될 예정 [해양] 루야 프로젝트 진행률 인식으로 4분기도 양호
한 이익률 기대, 다만 현재 수주 부진으로 블록, 혹은 LNG선 투입을 통해 매
출 하락을 방어하는 방안도 구상 중 [방산] 키윗과의 미국 합작 조선소를 통해 
콘솔탱커, 로로선 등 지원함 건조를 시작으로 전투함까지 확장 준비 [엔진] 국
가전력망 및 데이터센터향 발전 수요 확대, 하이퍼스케일러 직접협의로 성장
성 강화, 수주 확정 및 추진 물량 기준 28년 1.5GW, 29년 3GW 생산 계획 중

## Document 343: France_ double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and stable non-performing loans ratio (2026-10-08)

Source PDF: all report pdf/France_ double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and stable non-performing loans ratio (2026-10-08).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 8 October 2026

EcoFlash

France: double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and 
stable non-performing loans ratio

Business creations rose by 12% y/y over the first eight months of 2026 1. This is significantly higher than the 5.3% y/y 
growth recorded in 2025. This increase was driven by sectors linked to information and communication technologies.

Meanwhile, business bankruptcies rose by 5.3% y/y between January and July, according to data from the Banque de 
France (compared with the 3.6% y/y increase for the whole of 2025). The rise was more pronounced in the 1st quarter (+8% y/y) 
before slowing down. This slowdown could persist, as, according to Altares, payment delays have fallen to an average of 12.3 
days in the second quarter of 2026, compared with 14.1 days a year earlier (and 13.1 days for the European average).

Rising bankruptcies are more heavily affecting the services sector, which is also experiencing slightly stronger growth 
in business creations. This correlation is particularly noticeable in the retail sector, as business creations have risen by 13% 
since the start of 2026, while bankruptcies are up by 3%. In construction and manufacturing, business bankruptcies have been 
falling since the second quarter of 2026.

The average ratio of non -performing corporate loans has remained stable at a moderately high level for the past year  
(4.0% of total loans and advances in Q2 2026). In construction, where activity is more cyclical than in other sectors, the ra tio of 
non-performing loans has reached its highest level since 2019, standing at 9.5%.

The proportion of loans that have deteriorated but are not yet non-performing (“Stage 2” according to IFRS 9 terminology) 
is stable, standing at 15.4% q/q, and remains below its all -time high (16.1%). This indicator, which helps to anticipate trends in 
non-performing loans, is relatively encouraging.

Despite a significant increase since 2021, the economic weight of bankruptcies remains, on average, below its peak in 
Q1 2025 (0.53 compared with 0.61, respectively). This indicator generally allows bankruptcies to be weighted based on the size 
of the businesses. However, the economic weight of bankrupted small businesses is at a historically high level (2.11).

Take a look at our EcoCharts on trends in business creations and bankruptcies in France and the 
associated risks. It will be updated on a quarterly basis.

Thomas Humblot 
Economiste, team: Banking economics 
thomas.humblot@bnpparibas.com

Stéphane Colliac 
Head of Advanced economies, France 
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

1 For more details, see Business creations and bankruptcies in France: double-digit growth in business creations, lower growth in bankruptcies, 
and stable non-performing loans, 8 October 2026.

## Document 377: MiniMax创始人罕见公开访谈：将一直开源直到实现AGI，盈利靠压低推理成本不靠涨价

Source PDF: all report pdf/MiniMax创始人罕见公开访谈：将一直开源直到实现AGI，盈利靠压低推理成本不靠涨价.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-06
Pages: 29
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

MiniMax 创始⼈闫俊杰⾸次英⽂播客亮相，抛出惊⼈论断： AI 盈利靠降成本⽽⾮涨价，低
推理成本既是收⼊护城河，也能放⼤模型后训练规模。截⾄ 8 ⽉，公司 ARR 已超 8 亿美元，
B2B 占⽐逾 80% 。他还宣布将坚持开源直⾄ AGI 实现 —— 因为 " 智能应属于所有⼈ " 。
摘要：
中国 AI 公司正在尝试⾛出⼀条与硅⾕不同的道路。 MiniMax 创始⼈、董事⻓兼 CEO 闫俊杰（ IO ）
在⾸次英⽂播客访谈中表⽰，公司将坚持开源，直到实现通⽤⼈⼯智能（ AGI ）；⽽实现盈利的
关键，不是提⾼模型价格，⽽是通过技术创新持续压低推理成本。
在他看来，美国 AI ⽣态依靠巨额资本和算⼒资源推动模型能⼒突破，中国公司则必须在有限的算
⼒条件下提⾼效率。 MiniMax 希望证明，即使⽆法获得同等规模的顶级芯⽚，也可以通过模型架
构、基础设施和算法优化，缩⼩与美国前沿模型的差距。
这套思路已经开始反映在商业模式上。闫俊杰透露，截⾄ 8 ⽉， MiniMax 的年度经常性收⼊年化

运⾏率（ ARR ）已超过 8 亿美元，其中企业业务占⽐超过 80% 。他认为，低推理成本不仅可以让
客⼾以更低价格使⽤ AI ，还能让公司以更低成本进⾏强化学习和后训练，从⽽形成商业增⻓与
模型能⼒提升相互促进的循环。
与此同时， MiniMax 正在推进语⾔模型与视频⽣成模型的融合，希望让 AI 从⽣成⽂本和视频⽚
段，进⼀步发展为能够规划、执⾏并完成复杂项⽬的智能体。闫俊杰强调， AGI 不应该属于某⼀
家企业、某⼀个国家或某个组织，⽽应该属于所有⼈。
华尔街⻅闻整理要点如下：
1 、 MiniMax 将坚持开源，直到 AGI 实现。闫俊杰认为，开源不是商业变现的替代⽅案，⽽是推
动商业化和技术创新的催化剂。开发者可以基于模型开发新产品，形成反馈循环，帮助模型更快
迭代。
2 、低推理成本是 MiniMax 商业模式的核⼼护城河。闫俊杰认为， AI 盈利不应该依赖涨价，⽽应
该通过技术创新降低推理成本。成本下降既能吸引更多客⼾，也能让公司以更低成本扩⼤后训练
规模。
3 、中国和美国的 AI ⽣态正在形成不同的发展路径。美国依靠资本、芯⽚和算⼒资源推动前沿模

型发展，中国公司则更重视单位算⼒的使⽤效率。资源约束反⽽促使中国 AI 实验室在架构、基础
设施和算法上持续创新。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

从资源约束到效率优势： MiniMax 选择了⼀条不同的 AI 道路
对于 MiniMax ⽽⾔，资源约束并⾮⼀个可以轻易消除的短期问题，⽽是从创⽴之初就必须⾯对的
现实。
美国头部 AI 公司拥有更充⾜的资本、芯⽚和算⼒资源，可以通过⼤量实验寻找最优技术路线。中
国公司则必须更加谨慎地使⽤每⼀单位算⼒。这种差异逐渐塑造了 MiniMax 的组织⽂化和技术战
略。
闫俊杰将公司的⽬标概括为：最⼤限度地降低推理成本，同时让每个⼈都能够享受到前沿智
能。
他认为， AI ⾏业最终的竞争不只是模型性能的竞争，也是单位算⼒能够创造多少智能的竞争。
开源不是放弃商业化，⽽是建⽴更⼤的创新⽹络
在中美 AI 公司的发展路径中，开源与闭源已经成为⼀个重要分⽔岭。
OpenAI 、 Anthropic 等美国公司主要采取闭源模式，通过控制模型访问权限和 API 服务实现商业
化。中国 AI 公司则更加积极地采⽤开放权重模式，允许开发者下载模型，并在⾃⼰的硬件上部署
和修改。
MiniMax 选择站在后者⼀边。闫俊杰表⽰，开源并不是商业变现的替代⽅案，反⽽是推动商业

化和进⼀步创新的催化剂。
他以公司 H3 模型为例。按照他的介绍， H3 发布后的前两周，已经有⼤约 300 个基于该模型构建
的开放模型出现。
其中，⼀家美国公司 fal.ai 基于 H3 进⾏后训练，开发出 H3 Max ，能够在 3 秒内⽣成 5 秒的视频，
实现接近实时的视频⽣成。
这意味着，开源模型的价值并不局限于原始模型本⾝。外部开发者可以在其基础上进⾏优化，
开发出原本没有被设想过的应⽤。
闫俊杰举例称，开发者已经利⽤相关技术搭建了 24 ⼩时运⾏的 AI 直播系统，内容可以根据⽤⼾
需求实时⽣成。类似的能⼒还可能延伸⾄实时游戏、教育和其他交互式体验。
4 、 MiniMax 正在押注语⾔模型与视频模型的融合。公司⽬前拥有 M 系列语⾔模型和 H 系列多模态
模型，未来希望将两者整合为统⼀系统，让 AI 从⽣成内容升级为完成⼴告、影视、设计等复杂⼯
作。
5 、 AI Agent 的下⼀阶段不只是执⾏任务，⽽是⾃主设定⽬标并验证结果。闫俊杰认为，真正有
价值的智能体应当具备⻓期任务中的⾃主性。

6 、 MiniMax 的竞争优势不仅在于技术，也在于创始⼈的资本配置能⼒。 Scottish Mortgage 投资
经理 Lawrence Burns 认为，闫俊杰能够减少⽆效实验、作出关键技术选择，并以更少的资源取
得较好的研发成果。
7 、盈利仍⾯临价格竞争和前沿研发成本上升两⼤挑战。 Lawrence Burns 指出，中国 AI 市场竞争
激烈， MiniMax 仍需持续投⼊以保持技术前沿，市场空间虽然巨⼤，但盈利能⼒和利润率仍需验
证。

### Page 3

对于企业和政府客⼾，开源还有另⼀个优势：模型可以部署在⾃⼰的硬件上，从⽽更好地保护
数据和隐私。因此， MiniMax 希望通过开放模型权重，让开发者、⽤⼾和模型公司形成⼀个相互
促进的⽣态。
闫俊杰甚⾄明确表⽰，公司计划⼀直开源，直到 AGI 实现。
这并不意味着开源模式没有商业化问题。模型下载量和开发者数量最终仍需要转化为 API 收⼊、
企业客⼾和实际使⽤量。但 MiniMax 的判断是，开放⽣态能够扩⼤模型的应⽤范围，并让公司以
更低成本获得更多技术反馈。
盈利不靠涨价：低推理成本同时决定收⼊与研发效率
相⽐开源战略，闫俊杰对 MiniMax 商业模式的解释更加值得关注。
⽬前， AI ⾏业普遍⾯临较⾼的研发和算⼒投⼊，很多公司仍处于亏损状态。对于 MiniMax ⽽⾔，
如何在保持模型能⼒增⻓的同时实现盈利，是投资者最关⼼的问题之⼀。
闫俊杰的答案是：AI 的利润不是通过提⾼价格获得的，⽽是通过创新降低推理成本获得的。
他认为，低推理成本同时构成公司的收⼊护城河和利润护城河。
⼀⽅⾯，推理成本下降意味着 MiniMax 可以⽤更低价格向客⼾提供服务，扩⼤市场覆盖范围。并

不是所有企业都需要全球最先进的模型，很多⽇常办公、编程和业务流程，只需要性能⾜够
好、价格合理的模型。
如果模型性能达到业务需求，⽽价格明显低于竞争对⼿，企业采⽤ AI 的投资回报率就会提⾼。
另⼀⽅⾯，低推理成本还会影响模型本⾝的研发效率。
闫俊杰特别强调了后训练的重要性。在模型完成初始训练之后，还需要通过监督微调、强化学
习和数据合成等⽅式，让模型不断练习、纠正错误并提⾼复杂任务处理能⼒。
这些过程都需要⼤量推理计算。因此，推理成本越低，公司就越有能⼒扩⼤后训练规模，让模
型获得更多练习机会。
Lawrence Burns 对此给出了⼀个⾼尔夫球的⽐喻：两名球⼿都需要不断练习才能进步，但如果
其中⼀⼈购买练习球的成本更低，那么在相同预算下，他就能进⾏更多练习，最终更有可能成
为更好的球⼿。
MiniMax 的优势就在于拥有更便宜的 “ 练习球 ” 。这意味着，低成本并不只是⼀个定价优势，还可
能转化为模型能⼒提升的优势。
闫俊杰透露，截⾄ 8 ⽉， MiniMax 的 ARR 已经超过 8 亿美元，其中 B2B 业务占⽐超过 80% 。

不过， ARR 是按照当前收⼊⽔平年化计算的运⾏率，并不等于已经实现的全年收⼊。
对于 MiniMax ⽽⾔，理想的发展路径是：推理成本持续下降，吸引更多客⼾使⽤，收⼊规模扩
⼤，再将资源投⼊后训练和模型研发，最终推动收⼊增⻓快于成本增⻓。这也是公司希望建⽴
的增⻓⻜轮。

### Page 4

中国 AI 公司的另⼀种竞争⽅式：单位算⼒创造更多智能
在谈及中美 AI 产业竞争时，闫俊杰认为，两种⽣态系统正在形成不同的发展模式。
美国 AI 公司拥有更多资本、算⼒和芯⽚资源，能够通过⼤规模计算推动模型能⼒突破。中国公司
则需要更加重视每单位算⼒的效率。
他表⽰，美国的 AI ⽣态正在建⽴由巨额资本⽀撑的封闭系统，⽽中国的 AI ⽣态则正在建⽴由效率
驱动的协作体系。
他并不认为两种⽣态最终会⾛向完全相同的道路。这种差异不仅体现在商业模式上，也体现在
技术架构和研发⽅法上。
MiniMax 重点投⼊三个领域：模型架构、基础设施和优化技术。闫俊杰表⽰，公司从⼀开始就
希望模型能够在不同芯⽚上保持良好的运⾏表现，⽽不是完全依赖某⼀种硬件。
在他看来，算⼒越受限制，算法和推理效率的重要性就越⾼。
这种资源约束反⽽让公司的⼯程师和研究⼈员变得更加优秀。这也解释了为什么 MiniMax 将企业
⽂化概括为扁平、专注和节俭。
公司⽬前只有约 500 名员⼯，因此必须把资源集中在最重要的事情上。

闫俊杰提出了两项研发原则：第⼀，让事情尽可能简单；第⼆，始终建设基础设施。他认为，
直接推出产品可能更快，但⻓期来看，基础设施才是实现规模化发展的基础。
Scottish Mortgage 投资经理 Lawrence Burns 则认为，闫俊杰的⼀个突出优势，是他能够同时扮
演优秀科学家和资本配置者的⻆⾊。
在很多美国 AI 实验室中，研究⼈员可以利⽤充⾜的资本和芯⽚资源开展⼤量实验。但 MiniMax 没
有这样的条件，因此必须减少⽆效尝试，尽早判断哪些技术路线值得投⼊。
Burns 认为，闫俊杰能够更准确地判断技术⽅向，并在较少的实验选择中取得较好的结果。这不
仅提⾼了资本效率，也有助于吸引和留住中国顶尖 AI ⼈才。
从视频⽣成到完整任务：语⾔模型与视频模型的融合
MiniMax 并不打算只做语⾔模型或者视频⽣成模型。公司⽬前拥有两个主要模型系列： M 系列语
⾔模型，以及 H 系列多模态模型。
M 系列主要⾯向编程、智能体和⽣产⼒应⽤； H 系列则专注于视频⽣成，并能够处理图像和⾳

频。
闫俊杰表⽰，公司希望在未来将两个系列整合为⼀个统⼀模型。
这⼀战略背后，是他对 AI 应⽤形态变化的判断。
⽬前的视频⽣成模型能够根据提⽰词⽣成视频⽚段，但在理解复杂指令、根据反馈修改内容以
及完成连续任务⽅⾯仍有不⾜。

### Page 5

例如，在⼴告、电影和设计⼯作中，⽤⼾不仅需要⽣成⼀段视频，还需要不断调整镜头、修改
内容、理解创作意图，并最终完成⼀个完整项⽬。
这就需要语⾔模型的理解、规划和执⾏能⼒与视频模型的⽣成能⼒结合起来。 Lawrence Burns
认为，这种融合可能打开⽐单纯⽣成视频⽚段更⼤的市场。
当 AI 能够从⽣成内容升级为完成整个项⽬时，企业愿意⽀付的费⽤也可能显著提⾼。
与此同时， MiniMax 还在押注 AI Agent 。闫俊杰认为，下⼀代智能体的竞争重点，不只是能否完
成复杂任务，⽽是能否⾃主制定⽬标，并验证任务结果。
⽬前， MiniMax 已经将编程和 Agent 能⼒作为 M 系列模型的核⼼⽅向。他还特别提到，⾃⼰当时
正集中精⼒研发下⼀代模型 M3 Pro ，并认为它有望成为⼀项重要突破。
盈利仍⾯临两⼤挑战：价格战与前沿研发成本
尽管 MiniMax 已经展现出较快的收⼊增⻓，但盈利仍然是公司必须⾯对的问题。
Lawrence Burns 指出，今年上半年， MiniMax 收⼊接近增⻓四倍，但报告亏损规模也达到原来

的约三倍。
他认为，公司实现盈利⾯临两项主要挑战。
第⼀，中国 AI 市场的价格竞争⾮常激烈。
MiniMax 需要与 DeepSeek 等竞争对⼿争夺客⼾，⽽激烈的价格竞争可能压低整个⾏业的服务价
格。
第⼆，维持前沿模型能⼒的成本仍在不断上升。
随着 AI 模型不断进步，训练和推理所需的资源也在增加。这也是 MiniMax 在 7 ⽉份融资 20 亿美元
的原因之⼀。
在 Burns 看来，公司需要依靠精简的成本结构、正确的技术选择，以及能够⼤规模变现的市场来
应对这些挑战。
但他也指出，⽬前已经出现了⼀些积极变化：研发⽀出的增⻓速度开始低于销售收⼊增⻓速
度，企业业务收⼊也在快速扩⼤。
按照他的介绍，企业业务收⼊增⻓约⼋倍，⽬前占总收⼊的 63% 。⽽闫俊杰提到的超过 80% ，
则是按年化⼝径计算的业务占⽐。
企业客⼾的增加有助于改善商业化能⼒，因为随着模型性能提升，企业可以利⽤ AI 完成更多⼯
作，也更愿意为更⾼的⽣产⼒⽀付费⽤。
不过， Burns 强调， MiniMax 仍然是⼀项早期阶段的投资，最终结果存在较⼤的不确定性。这也

是基⾦控制其投资组合持仓规模的原因之⼀。
AGI 不属于任何⼀家企业： MiniMax 的最终愿景

### Page 6

访谈最后， Lawrence Burns 询问闫俊杰，如果 MiniMax 实现⾃⼰的⽬标，世界将会发⽣怎样的
改变。
闫俊杰给出的回答是：真正的问题不是 AGI 能否实现，⽽是 AGI 最终将让谁受益。
在他看来， AGI 不应该属于某⼀家企业、某⼀个国家或者某个组织，⽽应该属于所有⼈。
他认为，使⽤模型的⼈、基于模型开发应⽤的开发者，以及为模型作出贡献的研究⼈员，都应
该成为 AGI ⽣态的⼀部分。
这也是 MiniMax 将公司⼝号定为 “Intelligence with everyone” （与所有⼈共享智能），⽽不
是 “Intelligence for everyone” （为所有⼈提供智能）的原因。
两者看似只有⼀个词的区别，背后的含义却不同。
前者强调智能不仅应该服务于所有⼈，更应该由所有⼈共同参与和拥有。
对于 MiniMax ⽽⾔，开源不仅是⼀种技术策略，也是⼀种关于 AI 未来归属的选择。
⽽对于投资者来说，最终需要验证的仍然是另⼀件事：这种开放和低成本的路线，能否在激烈
的⾏业竞争中形成可持续的商业回报。

以下为访谈原⽂，由 AI 辅助翻译：
这场访谈由英国投资信托 Scottish Mortgage 旗下播客《 Invest in Progress 》推出，也是
MiniMax 创始⼈闫俊杰⾸次接受英⽂播客采访。
对话分为两个部分。第⼀部分由 Scottish Mortgage 投资经理 Lawrence Burns 与闫俊杰交流，围
绕 MiniMax 的技术路线、商业模式、开源战略以及 AGI 愿景展开；第⼆部分则由节⽬主持⼈
Claire Shaw 采访 Lawrence Burns ，进⼀步解释基⾦投资 MiniMax 的原因、公司的竞争优势以及
潜在⻛险。
对话嘉宾：
闫俊杰（ Yan Junjie ， IO ）： MiniMax 创始⼈、董事⻓兼 CEO ，机器学习和计算机视觉研究背
景，曾在百度深度学习研究院⼯作，是公司技术战略的主要推动者。
Lawrence Burns： Scottish Mortgage 投资经理，参与 MiniMax 投资研究和决策，重点关注
公司的技术路线、资本效率与⻓期商业化潜⼒。
Claire Shaw： Scottish Mortgage 投资组合总监，也是《 Invest in Progress 》主持⼈，负责
串联访谈，并在最后与 Lawrence Burns 讨论投资逻辑。
开场： MiniMax 为什么⾛出了⼀条不同的  AI 道路
Claire Shaw （主持⼈，节⽬旁⽩）：

### Page 8

公司以更低的成本、更快的速度进⾏创新和改进产品。

⼀个⽉内，它的下载量可能达到了 2500 万次。

因为我相信，如果能够把这些⽅法的规模不断扩⼤， AI 最终就会变得更加强⼤，也更加实⽤。

AI Agent ：下⼀阶段的竞争，不只是完成任务，⽽是⾃主设定⽬标
Lawrence Burns ：

MiniMax 如何实现盈利？低推理成本才是真正的护城河
Lawrence Burns ：

够重新投⼊研发，推动下⼀轮技术进步，最终实现盈利。
这是否也是你看待  MiniMax 商业模式的⽅式？
你⼜凭什么相信，公司⻓期来看能够实现盈利？
闫俊杰（ IO ）：
我认为， AI ⾏业的盈利并不是通过提⾼价格实现的，⽽是通过创新不断降低推理成本实现的。

### Page 15

我认为，低推理成本是我们在收⼊和利润⽅⾯的护城河。
这种效率使我们能够建⽴⼀种⾼度可持续的商业模式，其核⼼是  API 服务，⽽不是⼀次性的交
易。
举个例⼦，今年以来，我们的  ARR 增⻓⾮常出⾊。
截⾄ 8 ⽉份，公司的  ARR 已经超过 8 亿美元，其中  B2B （企业对企业）业务占⽐超过 80% 。
我认为，随着模型性能不断提升，同时我们继续保持价格竞争⼒，公司的收⼊增⻓速度⼀定会超
过成本增⻓速度。
这就是我们理想中的发展路径。
Lawrence Burns ：
你刚才提到了  ARR ，也就是年度经常性收⼊的年化运⾏率。
正如你所说， 8 ⽉份这⼀数字已经达到 8 亿美元。
为了让听众更好地理解这个指标，可以这样解释：如果按照 8 ⽉份的收⼊⽔平，将其乘以 12 ，就
可以得到约 8 亿美元的年化收⼊运⾏率。
我想回到去年 12 ⽉你告诉我的⼀个判断。
当时，你认为最好的中国  AI 模型与美国顶尖模型相⽐，性能⼤约落后 5% ，但成本却只有后者的
⼗分之⼀。
从那时到现在，双⽅在模型能⼒和价格⽅⾯的差距发⽣了怎样的变化？这⼜意味着什么？

闫俊杰（ IO ）：
我认为，真正重要的并不是某⼀个时间点双⽅之间存在多⼤的差距，⽽是双⽅能⼒提升的速度。
因为⽆论是美国模型还是中国模型，⽬前都还处于通往  AGI 的⾮常早期阶段。
⾸先，我想说，最后那 5% 的差距，实际上可能⽐最初达到 95% 要困难得多。
所以，我认为，⽬前剩下的这部分差距，可能还需要我花费⼀些时间才能解决。
但因为我们仍然处于  AGI 发展的早期阶段，所以真正重要的是我们能够以多快的速度进步。
对于我们⽽⾔，尤其是对于⼀家中国  AI 公司来说，算⼒始终是⼀种约束。
因此，从第⼀天开始，我们就知道必须尽可能提⾼推理效率。
事实上，在过去三年⾥，我们的技术进步速度已经⼤致达到了可⽐的⽔平。
我们关注的是进步速度，⽽不是某⼀个时间点的排名。
这也促使我们在模型架构和基础设施⽅⾯不断创新，⽽不仅仅依赖芯⽚本⾝。

### Page 16

我们相信，如果能够更加⾼效地使⽤算⼒，就能够开展更多的后训练⼯作，进⼀步提升模型能
⼒。
因此，我们始终在探索⼀种不同的⽅式来扩⼤智能规模。
Lawrence Burns ：
换句话说，是否可以这样理解：
美国的⼀些前沿  AI 实验室，过去拥有⾮常充⾜的算⼒资源。这让它们⾛上了⼀条不同的发展道
路 —— 它们在推动模型能⼒突破⽅⾯取得了巨⼤成功，但并没有像你们以及中国其他  AI 实验室
⼀样，从第⼀天开始就被迫将效率放在如此重要的位置。
闫俊杰（ IO ）：

例如，我们在  Transformer 架构⽅⾯使⽤了不同的⽅法，在后训练基础设施⽅⾯也采⽤了不同

所以你可以看到，中国不同前沿实验室开发出来的模型各有特点。

AI 不⼀定越强越好：真正的市场可能在性价⽐
Lawrence Burns ：
当我们思考  AI 市场机会时，我认为，你们如此重视每单位智能的价格，背后还有⼀个重要逻

辑。
随着技术前沿不断向前推进，整个  AI 能⼒体系也在不断扩展。
但实际上，我们并不需要全球最顶尖的前沿模型来处理各种普通消费者的问题，或者完成⾮常基
础的办公任务。
对于⼤量  AI 使⽤场景⽽⾔，重要的并不是拥有世界上最复杂、最强⼤的模型，⽽是拥有⼀个性
能⾜够好的模型，同时价格要合理，让企业采⽤它能够获得正向的投资回报率（ ROI ）。
⽽这实际上可能构成⼀个⾮常庞⼤的市场。
当然，也会存在⼀些需要顶尖模型的场景。例如前沿科学研究、癌症治疗，以及纽约、⾹港等地
的对冲基⾦所使⽤的复杂  AI 系统。
你是否也是这样看待整个市场机会的？
闫俊杰（ IO ）：
实际上，除了价格之外，还有另外⼀个重要因素。

### Page 17

更低的推理成本意味着，我们能够更加⾼效地扩⼤后训练规模。
因为在后训练过程中，⽆论是强化学习，还是数据合成，都依赖于推理。
所以，更⾼效的推理意味着能够开展规模更⼤的后训练。
我认为，在当前阶段，后训练正变得越来越重要。
未来某个阶段，我们甚⾄可能拥有全球规模最⼤的后训练系统。
我们相信，这将推动模型达到前沿智能⽔平。
开源与闭源：为什么  MiniMax 选择开放模型？
Lawrence Burns ：
美国和中国  AI ⽣态之间的另⼀个重⼤区别，是闭源模型与开源模型的使⽤⽅式。
OpenAI 、 Anthropic 等公司采⽤的是闭源模式，⽽开源模型在中国则更加普遍。
简单来说，采⽤开放模型的  AI 公司允许其他⼈下载并在第三⽅设备上运⾏⾃⼰的模型，⽽不需
要⽀付许可证费⽤。
此外，开放模型还允许⽤⼾进⾏更多微调，根据额外的训练数据调整模型权重。
那么，你如何看待开源模型和闭源模型各⾃的优势与劣势？
闫俊杰（ IO ）：
我认为，开源并不是商业变现的替代⽅案。
恰恰相反，开源是推动商业变现和进⼀步创新的催化剂。

例如，我们刚才提到的  MiniMax H3 模型。
实际上，在它发布后的前两周，就已经有⼤约 300 个基于  H3 构建的开放模型出现。
我认为，这是现阶段能够为我们的模型提供反馈、推动进⼀步创新的最佳反馈循环。
再举⼀个例⼦。
在这 300 个基于  H3 构建的开放模型中，有⼀个⽐较知名的模型叫  H3 Max ，它来⾃⼀家美国公
司  fal.ai 。
这家公司以  H3 为基础进⾏了后训练，⽽且实现了更快的⽣成速度。
它能够在 3 秒内⽣成 5 秒钟的视频。
也就是说，它已经成为⼀个实时视频⽣成器。
实际上，这种速度将带来全新的视听内容使⽤⽅式。

### Page 18

过去根本⽆法实现的事情，现在开始变得可⾏。
例如，开发者已经利⽤这项技术搭建了 24 ⼩时不间断运⾏的  AI 直播。
直播内容可以根据⽤⼾希望看到的东西实时⽣成。
同样的能⼒还可能让实时游戏、教育以及许多其他交互式体验成为现实。
第⼆个优势在于，当客⼾能够在⾃⼰的设备上部署开放权重模型时，他们就能够保护⾃⼰的数据
和隐私。
这对于企业和政府尤其重要。
所以，我认为，开源对于模型公司、⽤⼾和开发者⽽⾔，是⼀种三⽅共赢的模式。
因此，我们计划⼀直保持模型开源，直到  AGI 实现。
Lawrence Burns ：
谢谢，这⾮常有帮助，也很有意思。
过去六个⽉⾥，我们已经看到， AI 主权，或者说对  AI 模型的⾃主控制权，正在变得越来越重
要。
⼈们开始担⼼：如果⾃⼰使⽤的是⼀个由中⼼化机构控制的模型，那么当⾃⼰的业务建⽴在这个
模型之上之后，未来还能否持续获得访问权限？
另外，⾃⼰的数据究竟会发⽣什么？
这些担忧确实正在出现，也正如你所说，这给开放模型带来了优势。
MiniMax 的企业⽂化：扁平、专注、节俭

Lawrence Burns ：
接下来，我们谈谈  MiniMax 的企业⽂化。
我认为，可以⽤三个词来描述  MiniMax ：扁平、专注、节俭。
你们在组织架构上进⾏了相应设计，使公司能够快速应对  AI 领域发⽣的各种技术变化。
你们更倾向于内部培养⼈才，⽽不是花费极⾼的薪酬从其他公司挖⼈。
正如我们前⾯讨论的，你们也更偏好成本⾼效的解决⽅案，⽽不是依赖蛮⼒式的算⼒投⼊。
我想了解的是， MiniMax 的企业⽂化在多⼤程度上是由你们所⾯临的资源约束塑造出来的？你
⼜是如何让公司的组织⽂化与这些约束相适应的？
闫俊杰（ IO ）：
这是⼀个⾮常重要的问题。

### Page 19

我认为，我们的企业⽂化确实来⾃算⼒和资本⽅⾯的约束。
实际上，我们公司只有 500 名员⼯，所以必须专注于真正重要的事情。
在研发⽅⾯，我们有两项核⼼原则。
第⼀，让事情尽可能简单。
尽管我们拥有不同的模型，但我们的模型架构和基础设施都尽可能保持简单。
第⼆，我认为这⼀点也很重要：我们始终坚持建设基础设施。
有时候，建设基础设施的速度可能⽐直接推出产品要慢。
但我认为，这是公司未来实现规模化发展的基础。
所以，基于这两项原则 —— 尽可能简化⼀切，以及始终建设基础设施 —— 我们形成了现在的组织
⽅式。
我认为，资本效率、组织效率等其他特点，实际上都是这两项原则在资源约束下⾃然产⽣的结
果。
算⼒效率提升，会不会反⽽减少芯⽚需求？
Lawrence Burns ：
考虑到  MiniMax 已经成功降低了客⼾使⽤  AI 时的推理成本，⽽且更⼴泛地看，中国很多  AI 实
验室在算⼒使⽤⽅⾯也表现得更加⾼效，我想进⼀步了解：
这是否会让你对未来算⼒需求，以及英伟达这类公司⽣产的芯⽚需求，产⽣不同的看法？

因为，如果全世界都能像你们⼀样⾼效地使⽤算⼒，那么市场对计算硬件的需求可能会⾮常不
同。
你认为，这是否会影响⼈们对⻓期算⼒需求⽔平的预期？
还是说，你认为这更像是杰⽂斯悖论（ Jevons Paradox ）？
也就是说，当成本下降之后，使⽤量反⽽会上升。因此，⼈们始终会希望获得尽可能多的芯⽚，
因为他们可以利⽤这些算⼒做更多事情。
闫俊杰（ IO ）：
⾸先，我们的模型架构从设计之初，就考虑到了⽆论使⽤哪种芯⽚，都应该能够保持良好的运⾏
表现。
这是我们模型设计的基本原则。
我们真正能够做的，是在三个领域保持技术领先：
模型架构；

### Page 20

基础设施；
优化技术。
当硬件资源受到限制时，这三个领域的重要性都会进⼀步提⾼。
⽽这种约束也让我们成为了更优秀的⼯程师和研究⼈员。
当算⼒受到限制时，算法和推理效率的重要性就会越来越⾼。
中美  AI ⽣态：两条不会汇合的发展道路
Lawrence Burns ：
如果把视⻆拉得更⾼⼀些，考虑到你在中国  AI 发展过程中所扮演的重要⻆⾊，你认为我们应该

闫俊杰（ IO ）：
我认为，美国的  AI ⽣态正在建⽴由巨额资本⽀撑的封闭系统，⽽中国的  AI ⽣态则正在建⽴⼀
种由效率驱动的协作体系。
例如，美国公司拥有更多算⼒、更多资本，也拥有更多芯⽚获取渠道。
⽽中国公司不得不提⾼每单位算⼒所能创造的价值。
所以，我认为，两种⽣态并不会沿着同⼀条道路发展。
开放与封闭、效率优先与资源丰富，这些差异将决定两个⽣态系统最终会创造出什么样的产品。
Lawrence Burns ：

我们继续谈谈中国市场。
你们⾯临着来⾃本⼟⼤型科技公司的激烈竞争。这些公司雄⼼勃勃，资⾦实⼒也⾮常雄厚。
例如字节跳动、阿⾥巴巴，以及越来越多的⽣成式  AI 创业公司。
你刚才提到， ChatGPT 发布之后，中国很快就出现了⼤约 20 家⼤语⾔模型公司。
除了我们前⾯讨论的企业⽂化因素之外，你认为听众应该如何理解  MiniMax 相对于这些竞争对

闫俊杰（ IO ）：
⽆论是中国还是西⽅的⼤型科技公司，它们通常都需要在很多不同业务之间进⾏资源配置。
⽽我们只专注于⼀件事：
打造效率最⾼、能⼒最强的多模态  AGI 。

### Page 21

我们相信，效率本⾝就是智能的⼀部分。
你使⽤算⼒的效率越⾼，就能够把模型规模扩⼤到更⾼的⽔平。
开源模式也给了我们另⼀个优势。
例如，在中国，字节跳动采⽤闭源模式，⽽我们采⽤开源模式。
开发者可以基于我们的模型开发新的应⽤场景，并将这些成果反馈给我们，形成⼀个反馈循环，
帮助我们更快地改进模型。
我们还可以继续以  H3 为例。
字节跳动的  Seedance 是闭源的。
它能够⽣成⾼质量的视频，但⽣成速度相对较慢。
⽽基于  H3 构建的开源模型，则有可能实现实时视频⽣成。
这就带来了全新的东西。
从⻓远来看，我们希望把最优秀的语⾔模型和视频⽣成模型整合到同⼀个系统中。
我们认为，这将以统⼀模型的形式打开⼀个更⼤的市场机会。
这就是  MiniMax 的竞争优势。
AGI 最终应该属于谁？
Lawrence Burns ：
⾮常有意思。
最后，我想⽤我们节⽬每次采访创业者和企业时都会提出的问题来结束这次对话。
对于你⽽⾔，如果  MiniMax 实现了⾃⼰的雄⼼和愿景，世界将会变成什么样⼦？它会给世界带

来怎样的改变？
闫俊杰（ IO ）：
我认为，真正的问题不是  AGI 能否实现，⽽是：
AGI 最终将让谁受益？
因为  AGI 太重要了，所以它不应该属于某⼀家企业、某⼀个国家或者某个组织。
它必须属于我们所有⼈。
这也是我⼀直努⼒⼯作的原因：实现  AGI ，确保智能能够掌握在每个⼈⼿中。
真正的问题不是  AGI 能不能实现，⽽是谁能够拥有它，以及应该通过什么⽅式实现这⼀点。

### Page 22

我认为， AGI 应该属于使⽤模型的⼈、基于模型进⾏开发的开发者，以及为模型作出贡献的研究
⼈员。
他们都是最终塑造这个模型的⼀部分。
还有⼀个更宏⼤的问题：
如果世界拥有了更多智能，我们对世界、甚⾄对整个宇宙的理解是否也会发⽣改变？
我认为，答案是肯定的。
正是这种可能性激励着我不断前进。
Lawrence Burns ：
这⾮常有帮助。
所以，对你⽽⾔，你的愿景不仅仅是追求开放权重模型，⽽是相信这种⽅式能够将  AI 的⼒量传
播给更多⼈和组织。
因为模型是开源的，他们能够在此基础上继续开发，某种程度上也就拥有了  AI 。
他们不再需要依赖另⼀家公司持续向⾃⼰提供模型。
这是不是你真正看重的东西？
闫俊杰（ IO ）：
是的。
这就是为什么我们的⼝号是：
Intelligence with everyone （与所有⼈共享智能）。
⽽不是  Intelligence for everyone （为所有⼈提供智能）。
因为我认为，智能应该属于所有⼈。

投资经理复盘： Scottish Mortgage 为什么投资  MiniMax ？
Claire Shaw ：
Lawrence ，这真是⼀场⾮常有意思的访谈。
我尤其感兴趣的是  IO 提出的⼀个观点：中国和美国的  AI ⽣态并不会沿着同⼀条道路发展。
事实上，与美国竞争对⼿相⽐， MiniMax ⽆法获得同样多的顶级计算芯⽚，但这种限制反⽽可
能成为公司⻓期竞争优势的⼀部分。
不过，按照我们的惯例，节⽬最后还是要听听你对这项投资的判断。
⾸先， 2026 年初，也就是  MiniMax 在⾹港上市时，是什么让你决定将它加⼊  Scottish
Mortgage 的投资组合？
Lawrence Burns ：
我认为主要有三个原因。
第⼀个原因，是去年 12 ⽉我第⼀次⻅到  IO 时的感受。
我们对他介绍的情况，以及中国  AI ⽣态的发展都⾮常感兴趣。
按照  IO 的说法， MiniMax 已经能够以领先前沿模型⼗分之⼀的成本，实现约 95% 的性能。

其次，我们⾮常欣赏这种成本优势是如何融⼊公司基因的。
这种优势是在资源稀缺的环境中形成的，⽽这种起点对公司发展⽅式产⽣了深刻影响，也让它获
得了⼀种⽐我们最初认识到的更加结构性的优势。
正如你刚才提到的， MiniMax 并没有获得全球最先进  GPU 的同等资源。
因此， IO 从创⽴公司之初，就把重点放在如何从每⼀⼩时的计算资源中榨取更多有⽤的⼯作成
果上。
他们让芯⽚始终保持⾼利⽤率，并且从模型设计之初就把成本因素考虑进去。
这⾮常吸引我们。
因为这种低成本模式与我们在美国看到的模式有所不同，⽽且它在全球范围内可能具有⾮常⾼的
价值。
⾸先，并不是所有⼯作都需要使⽤最顶尖的模型。
其次，并不是所有⽤⼾和企业都像美国和欧洲的⽤⼾、企业那样富有，能够负担⾼昂的费⽤。
第⼆个原因，是  IO 本⼈。

### Page 24

他是⼀位备受尊敬的  AI 科学家，同时也被认为是⼀位⾮常优秀的资本配置者。
科学家和优秀资本配置者这两种能⼒集中在同⼀个⼈⾝上，是相当罕⻅的组合。
因此，我们认为他值得投资。
第三个原因是， AI 正在以极快的速度发展。
⽬前，⽆论是在美国加利福尼亚，还是在中国，都有⾮常有意思的技术进展。
我认为，如果你能够同时与这两个⽣态系统保持接触，就更有可能理解  AI 未来究竟会如何发
展。
如果你只观察其中⼀个⽣态系统，这项⼯作就会变得更加困难。
所以，我们认为，有必要在中国市场建⽴⼀定的投资敞⼝，以便学习和理解那⾥正在发⽣的事
情。
当然， MiniMax 给我们带来的股价表现也经历了⼀些波动。
正如你所说，我们在 1 ⽉份参与了公司的  IPO ，最初投资了⼤约 2000 万英镑。
公司上市后，股价出现了⾮常强劲的上涨。
这让我们有机会卖出价值 4000 万英镑的股票，相当于最初投资⾦额的两倍，同时继续持有公司
的股份。
以英镑计价，我们⽬前保留的持仓规模与最初投⼊的⾦额⼤致相同，仍然是 2000 万英镑左右。

所以，对我们⽽⾔，这是⼀笔不错的投资，也提供了⼀次⾮常难得的学习机会。
IO 的特殊之处：既是优秀科学家，也是资本配置者
Claire Shaw ：
这⾮常有帮助， Lawrence ，谢谢你提供这些背景信息。
我觉得⾮常值得注意的是， IO 在谈论⾃⼰的⽬标时，甚⾄将其与改变⼈类对宇宙的理解联系起
来。
在刚才的访谈中，你将  MiniMax 的企业⽂化概括为扁平、专注和节俭，并指出这种⽂化是他在
资源约束下追求宏⼤⽬标的产物。
我们不妨进⼀步深⼊讨论⼀下。
在你看来， IO 本⼈以及他所建⽴的企业⽂化，究竟通过哪些⽅式提⾼了  MiniMax 成功的概率？
Lawrence Burns ：
我还是想回到刚才提到的那个特点：
IO 既是⼀位优秀的  AI 科学家，也是⼀位⾮常出⾊的资本配置者。

### Page 25

观察整个  AI 实验室⾏业，尤其是美国的  AI 实验室，你会发现，它们在正式训练模型之前，往
往会投⼊⼤量资⾦开展各种实验，以判断哪些技术路线可⾏，哪些不可⾏。
它们拥有⾜够的资本和芯⽚资源，可以⽀持这样的做法。
但  IO 的重要性在于，他有能⼒成功缩⼩实验范围。
他会减少需要尝试的技术路线，作出更少但更重要的选择，从⽽提⾼资本使⽤效率，同时仍然取
得良好的结果。
因此，能够作出这些重⼤的技术决策就变得⾮常重要。
我们曾经与中国  AI ⽣态中⼀些⼯作多年的业内⼈⼠交流，他们认为，这正是  IO 的优势之⼀。
他能够⽐其他⼈更准确地判断技术将朝什么⽅向发展。
我认为，他作为科学家的声誉，加上他在资本配置⽅⾯的能⼒，也使他能够吸引中国顶尖的  AI
科学家加⼊团队。
正如我们从加利福尼亚以及美国市场了解到的那样， AI ⼈才⾮常稀缺，也极具价值。
因此，如果⼀家公司在吸引和留住⼈才⽅⾯拥有更强的能⼒，这最终就会成为⾮常重要的竞争优
势。
低成本推理的第⼆层价值：让模型拥有更多训练机会

Claire Shaw ：
接下来，我想进⼀步讨论  MiniMax 的商业模式。
我们前⾯谈到了公司如何将资源约束转化为竞争优势。
正如你所说，通过降低每⼀次模型回答问题的成本，公司就能够向客⼾提供更低的价格，从⽽赢
得更多业务。
但  IO 还提出了第⼆个不那么直观、却同样重要的观点：
通过保持低成本， MiniMax 可以承担更多模型初次训练完成之后的后续⼯作，⽽他认为，这个
阶段对  AI 技术进步正变得越来越重要。
在你看来，这⼀点对于  MiniMax 的投资逻辑有多重要？
Lawrence Burns ：
低成本战略是  MiniMax 所有⼯作的核⼼，⽽你刚才提到的第⼆层价值同样⾮常重要。
我们可以⽤⼀种⾮常简单的⽅式理解  AI 模型的训练过程。
⾸先，模型需要消化⼤量数据，从中获得知识，然后通过训练掌握这些知识。
接下来，还有⼀个叫作后训练（ Post-training ）的阶段。

### Page 26

在这个阶段，模型会反复尝试完成各种任务，不断练习，并从中学习什么⽅法有效、什么⽅法⽆
效。
过去⼏年， AI 领域很多重⼤的能⼒跃迁，都来⾃后训练阶段。
例如，模型现在能够编写代码、执⾏复杂任务，这些能⼒都与后训练密切相关。
所以，如果把这个阶段理解为模型的练习过程，那么它既昂贵，⼜⾮常重要。
因为模型每练习⼀次，就需要进⾏⼀次推理，也就会产⽣相应的成本。
我可以⽤⾼尔夫来打个⽐⽅。
假设有两名⾼尔夫球⼿，他们显然都会随着练习次数增加⽽不断进步。
但其中⼀名球⼿购买练习球的成本要⽐另⼀名低得多。
在其他条件相同的情况下，能够以更低成本获得更多练习球的⼈，通常会成为更优秀的球⼿。
MiniMax 就是这个例⼦中的那名球⼿。
MiniMax 拥有更便宜的练习球，因此同样的预算能够发挥更⼤的作⽤。
这就是低成本战略的核⼼价值。
因此，低成本回答不仅能够降低消费者和企业使⽤产品时的推理费⽤，还能够让产品本⾝得到更
多训练，以更快或者更具成本效益的⽅式持续改进。
Claire Shaw ：
很好，我很喜欢你这个⾼尔夫的⽐喻， Lawrence 。

盈利挑战：价格竞争与前沿研发成本不断上升
Claire Shaw ：
接下来，我们稍微转换⼀下话题，谈谈⻛险。
⽆论  MiniMax 的增⻓速度有多快，如果我们不认真考虑公司⾯临的⻛险，就没有尽到⾃⼰的职
责。
其中⼀个⻛险是，公司⽬前仍然处于亏损状态。
IO 认为， MiniMax 最终能够通过让收⼊增⻓速度超过成本增⻓速度来实现盈利。
但你认为，公司在实现盈利的过程中会⾯临哪些挑战？它⼜具备哪些条件来克服这些困难？
Lawrence Burns ：
我认为，这确实是⼀项挑战，我们不应该回避这⼀点。
今年上半年，公司的收⼊接近增⻓了四倍，但报告期内的亏损规模也达到了原来的⼤约三倍。

### Page 27

⻓期关注  AI 实验室公司的投资者会发现，这种投资和⽀出规模在⾏业内并不罕⻅。
但最终，公司仍然必须建⽴⼀个能够盈利的商业模式。
我认为，实现盈利有两个真正的挑战。
第⼀个挑战是中国市场极其激烈的价格竞争。
MiniMax 正在与  DeepSeek 等公司竞争，这并不容易。
第⼆个挑战是，想要始终保持在技术前沿，所需要的成本正在不断上升。
这也是  MiniMax 在 7 ⽉份融资 20 亿美元的原因。
那么，公司应该如何克服这些挑战？
我认为，答案的⼀部分就在于我们前⾯讨论过的⼏个因素：
保持精简的成本结构；
作出正确的技术选择；
在能够实现收⼊⼤幅增⻓的市场中扩⼤业务。
我认为，真正关键的是，随着模型能⼒不断提升，公司能够进⼊的市场也会越来越⼤。
⽬前，我们已经看到研发⽀出的增⻓速度开始低于销售收⼊的增⻓速度，这是⼀个令⼈⿎舞的信
号。
另外，我们也看到，公司的销售收⼊正越来越多地从消费者业务转向企业业务。
我认为，这将越来越有利于商业化变现。
因为随着模型能⼒提升，企业能够利⽤它完成更多⼯作，也就更容易愿意为这些能⼒⽀付更⾼的

费⽤。
MiniMax 在这⼀⽅⾯已经取得了⾮常强劲的进展。
我认为，企业业务收⼊增⻓了⼤约⼋倍，⽬前已经占到总收⼊的 63% 。
我想  IO 提到的数字是，按照年化运⾏率计算，企业业务占⽐已经超过 80% 。
因此，你可以看到，这种业务结构的变化正在实际发⽣。
但我认为，我们必须⾮常明确地认识到：
如果从整体上看， MiniMax 仍然是⼀项早期阶段的投资。
它具有⾮常明显的⾮对称性。
有很多事情可能朝着有利的⽅向发展，但最终结果的分布范围也⾮常宽。

### Page 28

这也是我们在投资组合中控制其持仓规模的部分原因。
MiniMax 的市场空间究竟有多⼤？
Claire Shaw ：
最后，我们来讨论⼀个问题。
IO 提到，提供更强⼤的  AI Agent ，并将  MiniMax 最好的语⾔模型和视频模型整合到⼀个系统
中，将打开更⼤的市场机会。
但我们都知道，这是⼀个变化极快的⾏业，因此很难准确判断未来会发⽣什么，以及这些变化将
在什么时候出现。
作为⼀名⻓期投资者，你认为  MiniMax 所提供的服务，未来能够形成多⼤的市场？
Lawrence Burns ：
对于这项投资，我有⼀个问题并不担⼼，那就是：
市场规模是否⾜够⼤？
我认为，只要公司能够成功实现⾃⼰的⽬标，市场显然⾜够⼤。
如果你能够以低成本提供越来越强⼤的智能，就⼀定能够找到市场。
⽽且，随着产品能⼒不断提升，市场规模也会持续扩⼤。
你刚才提到了语⾔模型与视频模型的整合，这就是⼀个很好的例⼦。
因为⼤多数⼯作实际上都需要多种不同技能的结合，⽽不是只需要⼀种技能。
今天的视频模型擅⻓⽣成视频⽚段，但它们并不擅⻓接受复杂指令。

它们也⽆法像专业视频剪辑师那样，根据要求对已经⽣成的视频进⾏修改。
这就需要语⾔模型所具备的理解能⼒。
⼀旦⼀个系统能够同时完成这两件事， AI 的应⽤就会从简单地⽣成视频⽚段，升级为能够承担
⼴告、电影和设计领域的完整项⽬。
⽽⼈们愿意为这样的智能和能⼒⽀付⾼得多的费⽤。
因此，我们预计，这⼀领域的需求将随着新模型的推出⽽逐步释放。
例如， IO 刚才介绍的实时视频⽣成能⼒，就是⼀个可能推动市场扩⼤的新应⽤⽅向。
所以，我认为，这项投资真正需要关注的重⼤问题，是竞争、盈利能⼒和利润率，⽽不是市场规
模。
我们对这个市场最终能够变得⾮常庞⼤，还是相当有信⼼的。

### Page 29

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

Claire Shaw ：
很好，我认为这是⼀个⾮常合适的结束点。
Lawrence ，⾮常感谢你今天分享的所有分析。
同时，也⾮常感谢  IO 接受我们的邀请，带来他⾸次英⽂播客采访。
这真的是⼀次难得的机会。
提醒⼀下，我们还将像之前的节⽬⼀样，推出这次访谈的 10 分钟精华剪辑，⽅便⼤家在时间有

## Document 395: Preview of 2026 New Zealand General Election (2026-11-06)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Preview of 2026 New Zealand General Election (2026-11-06).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-06
Pages: 13
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1
Westpac New Zealand Economics
• Polling points to a very close and unusually
fragmented election, with the outcome
likely to depend on the performance of the
Opportunity Party and whether any party
achieves “overhang” seats.
• While either Labour or National will lead the
next government, minor parties are likely to
have unprecedented representation in the next
government. This makes it harder to assess
the mix of policies that will emerge from

coalition negotiations.
• Compared to the status quo, a returned
National-led government with stronger
ACT and NZ First representation may be
more aggressive on some combination
of expenditure restraint, welfare and
immigration, resource development
and deregulation.
• A Labour-Green-Opportunity administration
would probably deliver more public-service
and social investment, a broader tax base,
stronger climate and labour regulation and
likely greater borrowing for infrastructure.

A centre-left government may find it hard to
resist pressure to lift public service salaries
and the minimum wage in response to fuel-
driven inflation.
• The fiscal starting point acts as a constraint:
the PREFU projects that OBEGALx will remain
in deficit until 2028/29 and achieving that
outcome will require exceptional spending
control in the current environment.
• Whichever government is elected will need
to make hard trade-offs, or raise additional

revenue or debt. The latter will be costly with
global investors requiring higher interest rates
to fund government (and other) debt.
• A key non-fiscal outcome of a change
in government would likely be the
implementation of the Labour Party’s
proposal to restore the RBNZ’s dual inflation-
employment mandate.
The election appears close, but the bigger
story is fragmentation.
New Zealand goes to the ballot box on 7 November
(although advance voting started on 26 October). The

campaign is taking place against a mixed economic
backdrop: activity has recovered somewhat, but
households remain sensitive to high living costs and a
weak housing market; the fiscal accounts have improved
relative to the Budget but still show deficits until 2028/29.
That combination gives the various parties room to
argue about priorities, but little room to avoid difficult
trade-offs. Additional spending – which may be difficult
to avoid in the current environment – will require either

additional revenue or a willingness to accept additional
debt issuance at a time when international investors are
seeking greater compensation to fund government debt
(and other).
The striking feature of this campaign is the reduced
dominance of the two major parties. Across the major
polls, neither traditional centre-left or centre-right
blocs can be confident of governing alone, and the
composition of the minor-party vote will determine both
who governs and how. Because the Opportunity Party

sits near the threshold, a movement of only a few tenths
of a percentage point could alter the allocation of several
seats. In addition, any “overhang” created by Te Pāti Māori
or the National Party winning more electorates than their
party vote entitlement could also move the majority line
above the usual 61 seats.
ECONOMIC BULLETIN
Preview of 2026 General Election.
27 Oct 2026 |  Westpac Economics Team | westpac.co.nz/economics | economics@westpac.co.nz

Kelly Eckhold, Chief Economist | +64 9 348 9382 | +64 21 786 758 | kelly.eckhold@westpac.co.nz

### Page 2

2
Westpac New Zealand Economics
Together with uncertainty about voter turnout –
especially considering changes in voter registration rules
– the outcome of this General Election is difficult to call.
With major-party support unusually low and the minor
parties offering distinct, sometimes internally conflicting
programmes, the shape of the next government will be
determined not only at the ballot box on 7 November,
but at the negotiating table afterwards. Moreover, if the

results are especially tight, the outcome may not be clear
until sometime after the official results are declared on
27 November. And a hung parliament – where neither the
centre-left nor centre-right bloc can command a majority
– cannot be entirely ruled out either.
How New Zealand’s electoral system works.
New Zealand’s electoral system uses Mixed Member
Proportional representation (or MMP). Voters receive
two votes: an electorate vote for a local candidate and a

party vote for a political party. The electorate candidate
with the most votes wins the local seat. The party vote is
the main determinant of each qualifying party’s overall
representation in Parliament.
Parliament normally has 120 seats. For the 2026
election there are 71 electorates, comprising 64 general
electorates and seven Māori electorates, with the
remaining seats ordinarily filled from party lists. A party
qualifies for proportional representation by receiving

at least 5% of the nationwide party vote or winning an
electorate seat. List MPs are then added to electorate
MPs so that each qualifying party’s total representation
broadly matches its party-vote share.
An overhang arises when a party wins more electorate
seats than its party vote would otherwise entitle it to.
The party keeps those seats and Parliament expands,
which can raise the number required for a majority.
Government formation is separate from the allocation of

seats: a grouping must demonstrate that it can command
confidence in the House and pass supply. That can be
achieved through a formal coalition, a confidence-and-
supply agreement, or other support arrangements. The
largest party is not automatically guaranteed office and
minority governments are possible (e.g. although the
National Party secured more than 44% of the vote in the
2017 election, a minority Labour Party-NZ First coalition
was formed, supported by a confidence-and-supply

arrangement with the Green Party).
The overhang risk is unusually important in 2026 because
current polling leaves both blocs close to, but often
short of, a majority. In addition, the fragmentation of
the electorate increases the risk that one of the major
parties wins more electorates than implied by its party-
vote entitlement. This is in addition to the likelihood that
Te Pāti Māori will achieve an overhang given that it is
running an “electorate vote only” campaign and so asking

its supporters to cast their party votes for other left-
leaning parties. Each additional overhang seat expands
Parliament and so lifts the threshold needed to command
a majority.
The direction of the effect depends on which party
creates the overhang. Extra Te Pāti Māori electorate seats
would add MPs aligned more naturally with a Labour-
Green administration and could make Te Pāti Māori
support or abstention more consequential. A National
overhang would add seats on the right and could improve

the arithmetic for a National-ACT-NZ First government.
Under the latest RNZ-Reid Research projection, for
example, an overhang would arise if National won more
than 32 electorates or Te Pāti Māori won more than two.
This makes the party vote strategically important and
explains the focus on Opportunity’s threshold position.
A vote share just below 5% would produce no list seats
unless the party won an electorate (which based on
limited polling presently seems unlikely). A result just

above 5% would give it a parliamentary caucus large
enough, on current polling, to become the “kingmaker”
and decide which side can form a government. While
the Opportunity Party says that it could work with either
a centre-left or centre-right government, as detailed in
the appendix its broad policy platform might imply that it
more naturally aligns with the centre-left.
What the polls imply.
Kiwipoll’s six-poll tracker, last updated on 20 October,

put Labour on 27.9% and National on 28.7% - a gap
of just 0.8 percentage points. More strikingly, the two
major parties together attracted only 56.6%. The central
message across all polls is therefore fragmentation:
neither major party is close to governing alone, and almost
half of the represented vote is distributed among parties
that would shape the terms of any governing arrangement.
On that average, the balance between the established
blocs is also extremely narrow. The model associated

with the tracker assigned 56 seats to Labour, the
Greens and Te Pāti Māori, and 61 to National, ACT and
New Zealand First. Neither side reached a majority in a
presumed 124-seat parliament. Opportunity’s average
support of 6.2% translated into seven seats, enough to
take either bloc to the 63-seat threshold needed for a
majority. The average therefore implies that the identity
of the largest party may matter less than whether
Opportunity remains above 5% and which grouping it is

prepared to support.
This is not simply a close two-bloc contest. The polling
average also suggests that the minor parties would enter
negotiations with unusually large leverage. The Greens,
ACT and New Zealand First would consequently have
more scope than usual to influence tax, expenditure,
regulation, immigration and resource policy, or the
allocation of Cabinet positions, making it harder to
determine the likely form of a final coalition agreement.

### Page 3

3
Westpac New Zealand Economics
That said, the leverage that these parties can apply on
their major party coalition partner is limited. The Green
Party cannot credibly threaten that it would form an
alternative coalition with the National Party. And neither
could ACT or NZ First credibly threaten to form an
alternative coalition with the Labour Party (while NZ First
has worked with the Labour Party in the past, it has ruled
out doing so on this occasion). Moreover, it is possible

that minor parties would be punished by the electorate
if a failure to form a government necessitated a second
general election, especially if the minor parties were
perceived to have made unreasonable demands or if the
electorate simply refocused on establishing a workable
parliamentary majority.
The Opportunity Party is the pivotal uncertainty because
its average position is above the threshold, but not
far above it. A result modestly below 5%, without an

electorate victory, would remove its votes from the
seat allocation and substantially change the arithmetic,
favouring the centre-right bloc. A result around its recent
6% polling average (or higher) would make the party
central to government formation.
Formally, National has ruled out working with
Opportunity, due to its objection to the latter’s tax policy.
For its part, Opportunity has said that it would talk first to
the party with the most seats, which on present polling

could be either Labour or National. However, as noted
previously, given Opportunity’s policy orientation, it
seems more likely that it would ultimately join a centre-
left coalition than a centre-right coalition.
To summarise, the bloc contest is close, and the route
to a majority depends on threshold and electorate
outcomes that remain uncertain. With that in mind, we
think that the following two scenarios are the most likely
outcomes of the election.

Scenario one: a returned centre-right
government, with greater minor party
representation.
The first scenario is that the existing coalition is returned,
albeit with the minor parties gaining strength and thus
additional leeway to pursue their policy agendas. We
assume that the coalition is not dependent on the
Opportunity Party to govern, which would be more likely
to occur if the latter fell marginally short of the party
vote 5% threshold needed to enter parliament in the

first place.
A renewed National-led government would begin with
considerable continuity. National’s fiscal plan retains
the current coalition’s focus on restoring surplus,
reducing debt as a share of GDP , keeping new spending
constrained and making greater use of planning reform
and private capital. It projects an OBEGALx surplus of
$4 billion in 2028/29, with net core Crown debt peaking
at 43.9% of GDP in 2027/28 and falling to 39.5% by
2030/31. National has ruled out new tax cuts during the

next term, saying tax relief should wait until the books
are back in surplus and surpluses are growing. But a
coalition in which ACT and New Zealand First held a
larger share of seats would be different from the current
more National-dominated administration. Unless its vote
share is particularly low, National would almost certainly
still provide the Prime Minister (sharing the role with NZ
First and ACT in the context of a three-year electoral
term seems quite unlikely – the latter will likely share the

Deputy role as they have in the current term). The three
parties share a common fiscal framework at the macro
level. However, National would have less ability to defer or
dilute its partners’ priorities.
Fiscal policy and the size of government.
ACT would press for a faster and deeper reduction in
the state’s footprint. Its fiscal plan proposes cutting
$9.5bn across the forecast horizon from spending outside
welfare and protected frontline services, reducing future

operating allowances by $400m a year to $2.0bn, and
moving non-welfare spending back towards 2017 real per-
capita levels. ACT says this would lower debt by $12.4bn
by 2031 relative to current forecasts. It also proposes
consolidating government into 19 departments and
18 ministers.
On its own, that agenda would pull a renewed
government beyond National’s more incremental
expenditure control. Coalition negotiations would likely
turn on the pace of implementation and the definition

of protected frontline services. The economic effect
would depend on whether savings represented genuine
efficiency gains or reductions in service capacity and
transfers. In the near term, a faster consolidation could
subtract from demand, while lower debt issuance
and a smaller structural deficit would strengthen the
fiscal buffer.
However, New Zealand First would push in a different
direction. Its programme combines selective tax relief
with active government intervention, regional grants

and strategic ownership. The tension within the coalition
would therefore not simply be between ‘more’ and ‘less’
government. ACT would seek a smaller and more market-
oriented state; New Zealand First would accept a larger
role where it supported national ownership, regions,
families or strategic industries. New Zealand First’s
proposal to reduce the corporate tax rate for SME’s to
20% (from 28%) may well be accepted by its coalition
partners during coalition negotiations – possibly phased

in over time – and would certainly be much cheaper
than the fiscal cost of some other policies promoted
by the party (notably compensating employees for
higher compulsory Kiwisaver contributions and the
nationalisation of the Bank of New Zealand).

### Page 4

4
Westpac New Zealand Economics
Tax, welfare and retirement.
ACT’s longer-run objective is a lower and flatter tax
system. Its immediate plan removes tax on qualifying
KiwiSaver investment earnings, ends the government
KiwiSaver contribution and returns net Emissions Trading
Scheme auction revenue to households through a
carbon-tax refund. Broader tax relief is deferred until
the books are balanced. ACT would raise the NZ Super
eligibility age by three months a year to 67 by 2035, while

allowing compassionate early access for people with long
careers in physically demanding work. It would also pause
contributions to the NZ Super Fund.
On welfare, ACT proposes tighter medical certification
and reassessment for disability-related benefits, a five-
year stand-down before migrants can access welfare,
and lower spending outside protected services. NZ
First would add its own eligibility restrictions, including
limiting NZ Super to citizens from 2029, while proposing

compulsory KiwiSaver enrolment from birth and a Crown
contribution. These policies would create difficult design
questions around transitional arrangements, hardship
and administration, but the broad direction would be
towards tighter eligibility and stronger conditionality.
Regulation, resources and labour markets.
A stronger ACT-NZ First presence would reinforce
deregulation in planning, employment and resource
development. ACT would seek property-rights-based

planning law, easier dismissal, a three-year minimum
wage freeze, a lower training wage for workers under
20 and removal of Te Mana o te Wai from consenting.
It would repeal the Zero Carbon Act. National’s own
RMA replacement and fast-track agenda would provide
substantial common ground.
NZ First would push more directly for regional and
strategic development: a special economic zone at
Marsden Point, offshore oil and gas surveying, expanded
mineral development, and the return of 50% of mining

royalties to producing regions. It also proposes breaking
up the power gentailers and has advocated bringing a
major bank back into New Zealand public ownership.
Both the National Party and NZ First have policies that
aim to force additional competition in the supermarket
sector, but these are opposed by the ACT Party.
Nonetheless, the result could be a government that is
simultaneously more deregulatory in consenting and
more interventionist in ownership and competition policy.

Immigration would also tighten. ACT proposes annually
reviewed work-visa categories, a Rural Workforce Visa,
stronger overstayer enforcement and physical-presence
requirements for permanent residents. NZ First proposes
replacing the Accredited Employer Work Visa with a
shortage-led Skilled Work Visa and holding a referendum
on a 20,000 annual cap on resident visas. National’s more
skills-focused approach would be under pressure from
both partners to place greater weight on enforcement,

settlement obligations and lower aggregate inflows.
Scenario two: Labour and the Greens,
supported by Opportunity.
The second main scenario that we view as likely is a
Labour Party-led coalition with the Green Party, with the
Opportunity Party supporting either inside the coalition
or via a confidence-and-supply arrangement. Depending
on precise outcome of the election, a confidence-and-
supply arrangement may also be required with Te Pāti
Māori to secure the necessary parliamentary majority.

A Labour-led alternative would represent a clearer break
with the current policy direction, but the size of that break
would depend on the success of the minor partners in
embedding their policies in the coalition agreement.
For its part, the Labour Party would aim to restore and
expand selected public services while remaining inside
its published fiscal framework. Its plan retains a $2.4
billion operating allowance for each Budget, targets an
OBEGAL surplus in 2028/29 and seeks to reduce net

debt below 20% of GDP over time. It would introduce
a 28% capital gains tax on post-1 July 2027 gains from
investment residential and commercial property, with
major exemptions, and use the revenue to help fund
health initiatives.
Labour’s central commitments include free prescriptions,
three free doctors’ visits, additional screening and
maternity services, 6,000 additional state houses,
public-transport fare caps, expanded apprenticeships, a
care-worker pay increase and partial reversal of planned

public-service cuts.
It would also restore the Reserve Bank’s dual price-
stability and maximum-sustainable-employment
mandate. As we have discussed in detail in a previous
note, in the current environment – dominated by supply
shocks – the return of the dual mandate could mean that
the RBNZ is prepared to accommodate a slower return of
inflation to target to reduce the impact on employment.
The Greens would shift the centre of gravity.
A strong representation of Green Party ministers in

the Cabinet would seek to move a coalition further on
redistribution, public ownership, housing and climate
policy. The party’s tax programme includes a 2.5% wealth
tax on net assets above $10 million excluding the family
home, a capital-acquisitions tax above $1 million with
exemptions, a 45% top personal rate, a higher company
tax rate for the largest firms, and a levy on large banks.
The stated objective is to raise about $5.1 billion net and
shift the tax burden from wages towards wealth and

large corporations.

### Page 5

5
Westpac New Zealand Economics
These taxes are needed to finance the Green Party’s
extensive promises on spending (see below). Formally,
the Labour Party has explicitly ruled out supporting the
Green Party’s tax policies to secure a Labour Party-led
coalition. Whether that pre-election commitment would
be reversed after the election is a matter of conjecture.
On spending and ownership, the Green Party proposes
free dental care and public transport, stronger income

support, expanded school lunches, more public housing,
a publicly owned renewable-energy entity and the
acquisition of supermarket stores to establish KiwiMart.
A centre-left government, especially with strong Green
Party representation, may find it harder to resist pressure
to lift public service salaries and the minimum wage
in response to fuel-driven inflation. Relative to PREFU
forecasts, this would imply additional expenditure that
would need to be met with either additional sources of

revenue or additional debt issuance.
Labour would resist parts of this agenda on cost,
implementation risk and fiscal credibility. The most likely
coalition bargain would therefore be a narrower set of
high-priority Green Party reforms, financed by some
combination of new revenue, reprioritisation and the
available Budget allowances.
The regulatory direction would be unambiguously
stronger. Labour and the Greens overlap on restoring
pay equity (which was not explicitly budgeted for in

Labour’s fiscal plan), strengthening worker protections,
improving rental standards, expanding public housing
and using government more actively in markets with
weak competition. Climate, freshwater and biodiversity
rules would tighten relative to the current government,
and mining on conservation land would face much
greater barriers.
Opportunity’s role, potentially outside Cabinet.
Opportunity could support a Labour-Green government,
either directly joining the coalition or through a confidence-

and-supply agreement. While the former would give
Opportunity seats at the Cabinet table, the latter
arrangement would let the party retain a distinct identity,
trading reliable support on confidence and budgets for a
defined policy programme. It may also provide more scope
for the party to “learn the ropes” with less public scrutiny,
given that none of its prospective MPs have ever been in
Parliament, let alone held Cabinet positions.
Regardless of what form the arrangement takes,

Opportunity’s landmark policies are neither a simple
extension of Labour nor of the Greens. Opportunity
proposes a “tax reset”, including a land value tax, with a
lower rate for rural land, alongside personal income tax
rates of 28%, 34% and 39%. The revenue raised would be
used to fund a “Citizen’s Income” payment to almost all
people, which would also replace several current social
welfare benefits. That said, Opportunity has conceded
that the land tax and Citizen’s Income are unlikely to be

implemented in the next Parliament given that it will
probably be the smallest of the coalition partners.
In negotiations, Opportunity would press for the
inclusion of its five bottom lines. These are (1) giving
the Commerce Commission more teeth and greater
independence to tackle excessive market power in
supermarkets and other uncompetitive sectors, (2)
regulating lobbying and capping political donations,
(3) developing a 10-year plan to "fix the health system"

alongside health professionals, with clear commitments
on funding, workforce and infrastructure, (4) phasing
out bottom trawling, working with industry, iwi and
coastal communities to fairly manage the transition,
(5) recommitting to Paris Agreement emissions
reduction obligations and protecting the Climate Change
Commission's independence. Opportunity’s other policies
include allowing net debt to exceed 50% of GDP if the
additional borrowing finances productive infrastructure.

It also proposes compulsory KiwiSaver contributions
rising to 6% for employees and employers (a policy it
shares with National), free public transport, stronger
competition enforcement, higher R&D spending and a
ten-year cross-party education plan. In a confidence
and supply arrangement Opportunity could reserve the
right to oppose other legislation it disagrees with, but
the arrangement would need sufficiently clear fiscal
guardrails to avoid repeated uncertainty over Budgets

and concerns about the stability of the Government.
The economic stakes.
The two scenarios imply meaningfully different policy
mixes, but neither government would operate without
constraints. The current fiscal forecasts already assume
a return to surplus and a declining debt ratio later in the
forecast period. Delivery depends on tight expenditure
control, revenue growth and an economic outlook
exposed to oil prices, global interest rates and domestic

productivity. Coalition agreements that materially add
to operating spending or reduce revenue would need
offsetting measures if the party heading the coalition
wished to preserve its stated fiscal track.
For monetary policy, the direct institutional contrast is
Labour’s proposal to restore the Reserve Bank’s dual
mandate. In practice, the larger near-term influence may
come through fiscal demand, labour market regulation,
migration and energy policy. A faster fiscal consolidation

on the right would tend to reduce demand pressure, while
a larger operating spending programme on the left would
increase demand pressure. Infrastructure investment
is somewhat different: if well selected and delivered, it
can lift productive capacity over the medium term, but
it can also add to near-term capacity pressure before
supply benefits arrive. And given the current interest rate
environment, the hurdle that must be crossed to justify
raising debt is higher than it has been for years.

protection areas.
Stronger emissions, freshwater and biodiversity rules;
publicly owned renewable-energy entity; phase out bottom
trawling and protect conservation land from commercial
exploitation.

development
Borrow for infrastructure and lift R&D spending to at least 2%
of GDP; technology-investment tax credits.
Te Rangatiratanga Trust and community/public investment in
housing, energy and ecological restoration; stronger locally
led development.

Minerals n/a Oppose mining concessions on conservation land and
prioritise nature protection, with tighter extraction
safeguards.
Agriculture Lower rural land-value-tax rate; R&D and innovation
incentives.
Lower nitrate limits, stronger freshwater and biodiversity
protections; family farms exempt from the acquisitions tax;
support regenerative and community models.
Regulation Strengthen competition enforcement, establish lobbying
rules and an anti-corruption body, and reform tax and

benefit administration.
Stronger labour, rental, environmental and competition
regulation; public ownership where markets are judged
inadequate.
Infrastructure Borrow more for productive infrastructure; separate

Public and community energy, KiwiMart, public housing and
major Māori/community funds; stronger public ownership
and climate alignment.
Immigration Pro-immigration settings including a lower skilled-visa salary

threshold and regional visas; detailed current package not
fully identified.
Residence routes for long-settled migrants; easier employer
switching; progressively lift the refugee quota to 5,000 by
2029; stronger family and humanitarian pathways.

## Document 465: 【國泰證期研究部】IC設計產業-機櫃密度攀升，帶外管理量價齊揚-20261103

Source PDF: all report pdf/【國泰證期研究部】IC設計產業-機櫃密度攀升，帶外管理量價齊揚-20261103.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-03
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

產業更新-IC 設計產業 
機櫃密度攀升，帶外管理量價齊揚 
What’s New –   AI 機櫃管理節點倍增，OOB 晶片量價齊升 
AI機櫃由多台獨立伺服器轉為整櫃系統，每個運算與交換托盤都需獨立管理，預估 NVL72整櫃
的 BMC用量達 8卡 GPU伺服器機櫃的六倍以上，GPU、DPU、液冷 CDU與漏液偵測也一併
納入管理範圍。產品規格同步升級，新世代 BMC支援 DDR5以對應新一代 CPU與 ASIC平台的
頻寬需求，開機安全晶片則整合 OCP開源信任根 Caliptra並導入後量子加密。控制 FPGA與
BMC韌體逐步整合為跨平台方案，管理網路也開始以光纖 PON取代機櫃內的 1GbE交換器，
OOB由單一晶片延伸至韌體與網路。

Trends/Outlook –   規格升級接棒，單櫃用量再倍增 
BMC 成長動能由伺服器出貨轉向規格升級與單櫃用量，即使雲端資本支出增速趨緩，管理晶
片仍具成長空間。規格方面， BMC 由 28nm AST2600、12nm AST2700 演進至 6nm 
AST2800，預估 AST2700單價較 AST2600高約 40～50%，AST2800預計 2028年中試產、
2029 年開始供貨，支援更多 CPU 核心與更強的資安功能，單價可望再往上走。用量方面，
NVIDIA機櫃由 NVL72走向 Rubin Ultra Kyber架構，預估單櫃 BMC用量倍增，Google TPU
與 AWS Trainium等 ASIC伺服器放量也同步帶動 BMC搭載。供給面，BMC交期一度拉長至
21～26週，預期 4Q26 E-glass載板完成認證、新增封測產能開出後，供給限制逐步解除。

Investment Summary 
1. BMC具備高客戶黏著度與寡占格局，加上占整機成本極低，供應商擁有定價能力，上游
成本上漲得以順利轉嫁。AI機櫃節點增加也同步推升 RoT、控制 FPGA與管理網路晶片
需求，預估全球 BMC市場規模 2026～2030年 CAGR約 18%，OOB由單一晶片擴展
為涵蓋韌體與網路的完整供應鏈。

2. 台廠主要受惠廠商包含信驊 (5274 TT)、新唐 (4919 TT)及達發 (6526 TT)，海外則為
Lattice與 Microchip。信驊 BMC全球市占估約 80%，AST2700接棒推升 ASP，新唐
BMC則成為 MCU以外的第二成長來源，2027年增幅預期高於 2026年。達發以 PON
切入 AI資料中心 OOB網路，資料中心 PON設備市場 2026～2030 年 CAGR預估達
52%。海外方面， Lattice 2Q26 營收年增 62%，控制 FPGA 搭載率持續提升，
Microchip PRoT產品線完整，受惠伺服器開機安全規格升級。

2

帶外管理獨立運作，BMC 居管理核心 
OOB 是獨立於資料網路之外的管理網路，在作業系統當機、甚至主機斷電時，仍能遠端開關機、
讀取溫度風扇與電源狀態並更新韌體。核心為主機板上的 BMC，搭配負責開機驗證的 PRoT／
SMC，以及負責上電時序與 IO 擴充的控制 FPGA，各節點再經機櫃內 1GbE 管理交換器上聯至
管理平台，例如 Vera Rubin NVL72 即配置 2 台管理用 TOR 交換器，連接各托盤 BMC 與
BlueField-4的 BMC。 
OCP推動 DC-SCM架構，將 BMC與 RoT集中於可插拔的管理模組，除降低主機板設計複雜度，
也讓管理晶片可隨平台世代獨立升級。韌體端以 AMI MegaRAC 與開源 OpenBMC 為主流，
Redfish則逐步取代 IPMI成為管理介面標準。

表 1. OOB 管理平面分層與代表廠商 
層級 功能 主要元件 代表廠商 
管理控制 遠端開關機、溫度風扇與電源監控、遠端 KVM BMC（AST2600／AST2700、Arbel NPCM8xx） 信驊、新唐 
開機安全 韌體驗證、SPI Flash監控、裝置認證 PRoT／SMC（AST1060／AST1040、 CEC173x／TS1800、MachXO5） 信驊、Microchip、Lattice 
系統控制 上電時序、IO擴充、訊號橋接 控制 FPGA、I/O expander Lattice、信驊 
韌體 BIOS、BMC韌體、Redfish管理介面 MegaRAC、OpenBMC AMI（已併入 Lattice）、開源社群 
管理網路 連接各節點 BMC、交換器管理埠、PDU 與液冷 CDU 1GbE管理交換器、PON（OLT／ONU） 網通設備品牌廠、達發 
資料來源：各公司資料、國泰證期研究部整理

機櫃整合加速，BMC 用量倍增 
8卡 GPU伺服器機櫃通常配置 4台伺服器，每台以主機 BMC搭配 GPU板 HMC，預估加上網通
設備合計約 10顆。NVL72則將運算與交換整合為整櫃系統，由 18個運算托盤與 9個交換托盤
組成，每個托盤都需獨立管理，主機板、DPU 與 GPU模組皆須配置管理晶片，預估每櫃搭載約
63顆 BMC與 18顆 mini BMC。下一代 Rubin Ultra 改採 Kyber架構，預期 2H27小量出貨、
2028 年放量。ASIC 伺服器在開發階段多以 FPGA 實現管理功能，進入量產後轉用 BMC，新唐
即自 2H25起於 CSP的 ASIC平台擴大導入。 
代理式 AI 需要大量 CPU 處理任務調度與資料前後處理，預估 2026 年全球伺服器出貨年增約
17%。此外，管理節點也由伺服器延伸至整個機櫃，資料中心交換器、電源櫃與液冷分配單元逐
步導入 BMC，高功率 AI電源並改以 CAN匯流排管理。

### Page 3

投資 顧問 
產業報告 
 2026/11/03

3

世代交替與調價，帶動 ASP 上移 
AST2700支援 DDR5，對應新世代 CPU平台的頻寬與安全需求，預期 2027年起各大新平台陸
續導入，預估占 BMC 出貨比重由 2026 年約一成提升至 2027 年近三成、2028 年近五成。
NVIDIA Vera Rubin 整櫃則預期延續採用 AST2600，交換器 BMC亦於 2027年全面導入。新唐
規劃 4Q26推出新一代 BMC與 CMS，同樣支援 DDR5，惟新品須經客戶平台驗證，短期營收仍
以既有 Arbel系列為主，並以整合 BMC子系統的 NPCM8mnx SiP降低客戶設計門檻。 
成本面，T-glass載板、封測與成熟製程同步漲價，信驊 4月調漲 BMC價格雙位數百分比，預期
12月全產品線再調漲雙位數。新唐除 4月調漲自有 6吋晶圓價格外，也因 DRAM與 Good Die
漲價向客戶發出漲價通知。

轉換成本高築，BMC 寡占格局穩固 
BMC是伺服器韌體與管理軟體的基礎，韌體須針對特定晶片開發，包括 Linux核心驅動、I2C／
eSPI 等周邊介面、KVM 影像引擎與加密引擎，不同廠商的 BMC 無法共用同一套韌體。雲端業
者在 OpenBMC 上完成客製化後，韌體還須與 BIOS、PRoT 組成的安全開機驗證鏈，以及資料
中心的監控告警、遠端更新流程深度整合，整個機群以同一套韌體統一管理。若導入第二家
BMC，除須重寫驅動並重新驗證外，還得同時維護兩套韌體版本與資安修補，維運成本與出錯
風險同步增加，因此客戶多半整個平台採用同一家 BMC。新平台的 BMC也須隨 CPU平台同步
設計，驗證週期長達兩到三年，一旦導入，通常延續整個產品世代並延伸至次世代平台。 
產業結構方面，BMC 市場由信驊主導，其餘主要由新唐瓜分，新進者難以在短期內累積足夠的
平台驗證實績。BMC占整台伺服器成本不到 1%，相較於停機或重新驗證的代價，客戶對價格敏
感度低，信驊 4月調漲 BMC價格後，2Q26毛利率仍達 72.0%，上游成本漲幅可完整轉嫁。 
出貨方面，預估信驊 BMC出貨由 2025年約 1,800萬顆成長至 2028年約 4,700萬顆，年複合成
長率約 38%，且高單價 AST2700占比同步提升，出貨與單價雙引擎推升營收。 
圖 1. 信驊 BMC 出貨量預估

資料來源：信驊，國泰證期研究部預估

管理網路光纖化，PON 切入資料中心 
OOB網路傳統做法為每櫃配置 1～2台 1GbE管理交換器，以銅線連接 BMC、交換器管理埠、
PDU與液冷 CDU，隨節點數增加，交換器數量、布線與機櫃空間同步膨脹。PON以光纖搭配免
供電的無源分光器取代機櫃內交換器，依客戶測試可節省約 99%管理機櫃空間，並減少耗電與維
運節點。 
達發將 10G PON 導入 AI 資料中心 OOB 架構，預期相關市場 2026～2028 年逐年翻倍，25G 
PON也預期 2027年量產，乙太網 10G PHY則隨 BMC出貨切入 OOB應用。惟資料中心管理網
路長期採用乙太網路，PON導入速度仍取決於大型 CSP能否形成產業規範。

項目 8 卡 GPU 伺服器機櫃 AI 機櫃（NVL72） 
架構 約 4台伺服器，各台獨立管理 18個運算托盤＋9個交換托盤，整櫃管理 
管理對象 CPU、GPU、記憶體、風扇與電源 另含 NVLink交換托盤、DPU、液冷 CDU與漏液偵測 
主要 BMC AST2600搭配 HMC AST2600，預估每櫃約 63顆 BMC＋18顆 mini BMC 
安全需求 PFR、TPM PRoT／SMC、Caliptra、PQC 
管理網路 單台 1GbE管理交換器、銅線 管理交換器數量擴增，PON以無源分光器取代交換器

龍頭地位穩固，新進者各取利基 
BMC方面，信驊 AST2700世代領先，次世代產品規劃亦已展開。新唐為 CSP導入第二供應源的
主要受惠者，雖 BMC占營收比重仍低，惟現有客戶合作已涵蓋次世代產品，並啟動新 CSP客戶
送樣，在 BMC黏著度高的特性下，一旦導入即可長期貢獻，可望隨 CSP擴大第二供應源持續提
升市占。 
安全與控制方面，Lattice受惠每台伺服器的控制 FPGA由 2024年約 1.5顆增至 2026年逾 3顆，
完成 AMI 併購後，FPGA 也可搭配 MegaRAC 韌體跨 CPU、GPU 與 ASIC 平台整套銷售。
Microchip資料中心營收 FQ1'27年增 97.8%，PRoT、系統管理與電源晶片同步受惠 AI機櫃建
置，預估 2026年資料中心營收約 US$10億。管理網路方面，達發 PON屬架構變革，若 CSP擴
大採用，將成為台廠中少數由網路端受惠 OOB的廠商。
